```
1	# STL to STEP Conversion Utility - Development Plan
2	
3	## Project Overview
4	
5	**brepper** (B-Rep from Mesh) - A utility to convert triangulated STL meshes from CAD exports into parametric STEP files with fitted analytic and freeform surfaces.
6	
7	## Assumptions
8	
9	- Assumes the vertices of the mesh lie precisely on the CAD surfaces, modulo numerical error/stability.
10	- Assumes transitions between shapes always have a seam.
11	- Assumes manifold mesh (each edge shared by exactly 2 faces).
12	- Assumes consistent face orientation (all normals point outward).
13	
14	Fails noisily if any of the assumptions are violated. Possible future work: offer a repair mode.
15	
16	## Dependencies
17	
18	| Library | Purpose | Version |
19	|---------|---------|---------|
20	| OpenCASCADE (OCCT) | STL import, B-Rep modeling, surface fitting, STEP export | latest |
21	
22	Use the opencascade binding in ../opencascade-rs/crates/opencascade-sys, including porting instructions in PORTING.md and details on the FFI mapping in ../opencascade-rs/crates/opencascade-binding-generator/README.md. It's a reasonably complete binding of the C++ API of the opencascade library which resides in ../opencascade-rs/crates/occt-sys/OCCT, with source code in src/ and documentation in dox/.
23

89	## Open Questions
90	
91	- Links between data structures (face => edge, edge => vertex, etc): Use indices or pointers or references or other?
92	- Data structure management: Vectors? Reference counting?
93	
94	*Comment: Indices into vectors are generally the right choice for this domain—they're stable under serialization, easily debuggable, and avoid lifetime complexity. Pointers/references become problematic when hypotheses are deleted or vectors reallocate. Consider using "generation counters" or a slot-map pattern if you need to detect stale indices.*
95	
96	## Architecture
97	
98	### Stage 1: Mesh Input & Preprocessing
99	
100	#### 1.1 Read STL File
101	Read the input STL file and generate an in-memory representation of the triangle mesh, with fields for future stages to traverse the mesh and fit shapes to sets of mesh faces. Weld vertices by position (with tolerance) to build connectivity.
102	- **Library**: OCCT \`RWStl::ReadFile\` (static function, via \`rw_stl::read_file_charptr_progressrange_2\` in the Rust bindings)
103	- **Input**: Binary or ASCII STL file
104	- **Output**: \`ConnectedMesh\`, storing:
105	    - \`vertices: Vec<MeshVertex>\` — Mesh vertices with double-precision 3D coordinates (\`x\`, \`y\`, \`z: f64\`).
106	    - \`faces: Vec<MeshFace>\` — Mesh faces with:
107	        - \`vertex_count: u8\` — 3 for triangles, 4 for quads. Always 3 after initial STL input; stage 1.3 may merge coplanar triangle pairs into quads.
108	        - \`vertex_indices: [usize; 4]\` — Indices of vertices, ordered by right-hand rule.
109	        - \`neighbors: [i32; 4]\` — Index of the mesh face across each edge, or -1 if none. Filled in stage 1.2.
110	        - \`normal: Option<[f64; 3]>\` — Mesh face normal, computed from vertices in stage 1.2. \`None\` for degenerate faces. (Changed from \`gp_Dir\` in original plan — native Rust arrays avoid OCCT binding complexity and keep the mesh data structure self-contained.)
111	        - \`planar_hypothesis: i32\` — Index of active planar hypothesis, or \`NO_HYPOTHESIS\` (-1) if none, or \`UNDEDUCED_PLANAR_HYPOTHESIS\` (-2) if not yet deduced.
112	        - \`conical_hypothesis: i32\` — Index of active conical/cylindrical hypothesis, or \`NO_HYPOTHESIS\` (-1) if none, or \`UNDEDUCED\` (-2) if not yet deduced. Cylinders and cones are unified: both use this field with \`ConicalHypothesis\` (cylinders have \`half_angle == 0\`).
113	        - \`spherical_hypothesis: i32\` — Index of active spherical hypothesis, or \`NO_HYPOTHESIS\` (-1) if none, or \`UNDEDUCED\` (-2) if not yet deduced.
114	    - \`stats: MeshValidationStats\` — Statistics described in stage 1.2.
115	
116	Note: hypotheses vectors (planar, conical, spherical, toroidal) are stored in \`Stage2Output\`, not in \`ConnectedMesh\`. The mesh stores only per-face hypothesis indices. Cylinders and cones share the same hypothesis vector (\`conical_hypotheses\`) and per-face field (\`conical_hypothesis\`), with \`half_angle == 0\` distinguishing cylinders from true cones.
117	
118	For now, each face can belong to a single hypothesis of each type. Hopefully that's sufficient since hypotheses should nearly exactly match the vertices, but it's possible that in the future we may need to keep a set of candidate hypothesis indices per type, then select the best one in stage 2.6.
119	
120	**Vertex welding** uses a spatial hash grid: each vertex is bucketed by integer coordinates \`(round(x/tol), round(y/tol), round(z/tol))\`. To find matches, adjacent cells (3×3×3 neighborhood) are checked for distance ≤ tolerance. This gives O(1) average lookup. The weld tolerance is derived from \`--vertex-tolerance\` as \`min(1e-9, vertex_tolerance * 0.01)\`, so welding is much tighter than the surface-fitting tolerance.
121	
122	#### 1.2 Mesh Validation
123	Traverse the faces of the mesh, collecting statistics, validating the geometry, and populating normals and neighbors.
124	- Collect stats into \`MeshValidationStats\` and optionally print: \`mesh_faces\`, \`mesh_vertices\`, \`mesh_edges_open\` (edges with only 1 incident face), \`mesh_edges_non_manifold\` (edges with >2 incident faces), \`mesh_edges_inconsistent_orientation\`, \`mesh_faces_degenerate\`, \`connected_shells\`, \`solids\`, \`voids_within_solids\`.
125	- Compute and populate mesh face normals using Newell's method (handles both triangles and quads with consistent winding).
126	- Compute mesh face neighbors based on shared mesh edges. For each edge (vertex pair), collect all face uses. For manifold edges (exactly 2 uses), set both faces as neighbors. Also check orientation consistency: properly oriented adjacent faces should traverse the shared edge in opposite directions.
127	- Count connected shells via BFS over a face adjacency graph. A shell is counted as a "solid" if all its edges are manifold (exactly 2 faces per edge). (Simplified from the original plan which suggested ray casting or signed volume analysis — the current heuristic is sufficient for well-formed meshes. Voids within solids are tracked in the stats struct but not yet computed.)
128	- With the \`--compare\` flag: check that all vertices are within \`--vertex-tolerance\` of the STEP shape, and that all face centroids are within \`--surface-tolerance\`. Uses \`BRepExtrema_DistShapeShape\` for bounded distance (respects face trimming). Reports the worst vertex and worst centroid with their distances.
129	
130	Validation checks, in priority order (first failure is reported):
131	1. **Degenerate faces**: faces with <3 vertices or zero-area normals.
132	2. **Non-manifold edges**: edges shared by >2 faces.
133	3. **Inconsistent orientation**: adjacent faces that traverse a shared edge in the same direction (indicating flipped normals within a shell).
134	4. **Inverted normals**: for each closed shell, compute the signed volume via the divergence theorem (sum of signed tetrahedra formed by each face and the origin: V = (1/6) Σ v0·(v1×v2)). A negative signed volume is legitimate for an internal void shell (normals point out of the material, i.e. into the cavity): such shells are accepted — and counted in \`voids_within_solids\` — when their bounding box is contained in some closed positive shell's box. Otherwise a negative volume means all face normals point inward (consistently inverted winding); this catches meshes where orientation is internally consistent (check 3 passes) but globally inverted.
135	5. **Self-intersections**: intentionally deferred to a later stage where richer geometric predicates are available and O(N²) triangle checks can be avoided.
136	
137	#### 1.3 Coplanar Triangle Fusion (Triangle → Quad Merging)
138	Merge adjacent coplanar triangle pairs that share a diagonal edge into quads. This is important because CAD tessellators (including CodeCAD/OCCT and Onshape) typically produce quad-strip patterns on curved surfaces (cylinders, spheres, cones). Each quad is split into two coplanar triangles sharing a diagonal. Without fusion, stage 2.1 groups these into 2-face planar hypotheses, which complicates the "single-face = curved surface candidate" criterion in stages 2.2 and 2.3.
139	
140	**Algorithm (implemented):**
141	
142	The algorithm operates in three phases: identify merge pairs, apply merges, then compact the face array.
143	
144	*Phase 1 — Identify merge pairs:*
145	- Iterate faces in index order. For each unmerged triangle face \`fi\`, examine each edge.
146	- For each manifold edge shared with a neighbor \`ni\` (where \`ni > fi\` to avoid processing edges twice), both must be unmerged triangles with valid normals.
147	- Find the 4th vertex \`b\` from \`ni\` (the vertex not on the shared edge). Three vertices (\`s0\`, \`s1\`, \`a\`) define face \`fi\`'s plane.
148	- **Coplanarity check**: compute the signed distance from \`b\` to \`fi\`'s plane (using \`fi\`'s unit normal and one of its vertices). If \`|distance| <= vertex_tolerance\`, the vertex is coplanar within the data's numerical accuracy. (Using \`vertex_tolerance\` directly is correct since it represents the approximate precision of STL vertex positions — values within this tolerance are indistinguishable from coplanar.)
149	- **Convexity check**: verify the quad \`[s1, a, s0, b]\` is strictly convex by checking that the cross product at each of the 4 consecutive vertex triples agrees in direction with the face normal (positive dot product). Non-convex quads would break Newell's method for normal computation.
150	- If both checks pass, mark both \`fi\` and \`ni\` as merged and record the pair. Each triangle participates in at most one merge (greedy: first valid edge wins for each face).
151	
152	*Phase 2 — Apply merges:*
153	- For each merge pair \`(fi, ni)\`: transform \`fi\` into a quad with \`vertex_count = 4\`, vertex order \`[s1, a, s0, b]\` (right-hand rule consistent with \`fi\`'s winding), and neighbors assembled from both triangles' non-shared edges. Recompute the normal via Newell's method. Update \`ni\`'s former neighbors to point to \`fi\`. Mark \`ni\` as deleted (\`vertex_count = 0\`).
154	
155	*Phase 3 — Compact:*
156	- Remove deleted faces, build an old→new index mapping, remap all neighbor references.
157	- Update \`mesh_faces\` stat and record \`mesh_quads\` count.
158	
159	**Constraints:**
160	- Each triangle participates in at most one merge (greedy: first valid merge wins).
161	- Only merge if the result is a convex quad — this ensures Newell's method produces a correct normal.
162	- Triangles at surface boundaries (where two curved surfaces meet, or a curved surface meets a plane) will generally NOT be coplanar with their neighbor across that boundary, so they correctly remain as triangles.
163	- On finely-tessellated curved surfaces (spheres, tori), adjacent quads may be nearly coplanar within \`vertex_tolerance\`. These additional merges are valid — they reduce face count and create more single-face candidates for curved surface hypothesis seeding.
164	- Sphere poles: triangles near poles may not have a coplanar neighbor (they border 5 or 6 other triangles in a fan pattern, not a quad strip). These correctly remain as triangles and become single-face planar hypotheses — good candidates for spherical hypothesis seeding.
165	
166	---
167	
168	### Stage 2: Surface Fitting
169	
170	**Precision note**: Mesh vertex positions, normals, and all hypothesis parameters use double-precision (\`f64\`) throughout. Surface fitting arithmetic and BFS distance computations are all in double precision.
171	
172	#### Generic BFS Hypothesis Generation
173	
174	Stages 2.1–2.5 share a common algorithm structure for deducing surface hypotheses from the mesh. This section describes the generic algorithm; each stage section below describes how it is parameterized. The Rust implementation uses the \`SurfaceType\` trait in \`bfs.rs\` to share code across surface types.
175	
176	The algorithm operates on a single surface type at a time (planar, conical/cylindrical, spherical, toroidal). For each surface type, the algorithm iterates over all faces that don't yet have a hypothesis of that type, attempting to grow a hypothesis from each face.
177	
178	**Hypothesis data structure:**
179	Each surface type defines a hypothesis struct with type-specific parameters (see per-stage sections) plus common fields:
180	- \`faces: Vec<usize>\` — Mesh face indices in this hypothesis.
181	- \`vertices: Vec<usize>\` — Mesh vertex indices on this surface.
182	- \`error_max: f64\` — Maximum absolute distance from any vertex to the fitted surface.
183	- \`error_abs_sum: f64\` — Sum of absolute vertex-to-surface distances.
184	
185	Each \`MeshFace\` has a hypothesis index field per surface type (e.g., \`planar_hypothesis\`, \`conical_hypothesis\`, \`spherical_hypothesis\`, \`toroidal_hypothesis\`), initialized to \`UNDEDUCED\` (-2) at the start of each stage. Conical and cylindrical surfaces share the \`conical_hypothesis\` field (cylinders have \`half_angle == 0\`).
186	
187	**Algorithm: \`generate_hypotheses<S: SurfaceType>(mesh, config)\` — runs the full hypothesis generation for one surface type:**
188	
189	1. Initialize all hypothesis fields for this surface type to \`UNDEDUCED\`.
190	2. For each face \`fi\` in index order where the face is eligible (hypothesis field == \`UNDEDUCED\`, plus any surface-type-specific eligibility checks):
191	    a. **Seed generation**: call \`S::generate_seeds()\` to find all valid seeds anchored at \`fi\` (see per-stage sections for seed types). For each candidate seed:
192	        - Collect the seed faces' vertices.
193	        - Perform a surface fit from the seed vertices using \`S::fit_surface()\`.
194	        - **Seed vertex tolerance**: check all seed face vertices within \`--vertex-tolerance\` of the fitted surface. Reject seed if any vertex exceeds this.
195	        - **Seed surface tolerance**: check all seed face centroids within \`--surface-tolerance\` of the fitted surface. Reject seed if any fails.
196	        - **Seed angular tolerance**: check dihedral angles between all pairs of adjacent seed faces. Reject seed if any exceeds \`--angular-tolerance\`.
197	        - Determine convexity from the seed faces.
198	    b. **BFS expansion** (per valid seed): using temporary data structures (local face list, vertex set — mesh hypothesis fields are NOT mutated during trials):
199	        - Initialize BFS queue with all seed faces.
200	        - Pop faces from the queue. For each neighbor \`ni\` of the popped face:
201	            - Skip if \`ni\` is not eligible for this surface type (already assigned a hypothesis, or fails surface-type-specific eligibility).
202	            - Skip if \`ni\` is already claimed by this trial.
203	            - **Angular tolerance check**: for each of \`ni\`'s mesh neighbors that is already claimed by this trial, compute the dihedral angle. If any exceeds \`--angular-tolerance\`, skip \`ni\`.
204	            - **Convexity check**: call \`S::convexity_check()\`. If it fails, skip \`ni\`.
205	            - **Surface distance check**: compute distance from each vertex of \`ni\` to the fitted surface via \`S::vertex_distance()\`, and from \`ni\`'s centroid via \`S::centroid_distance()\`.
206	                - If all vertices are within \`--vertex-tolerance\` and centroid within \`--surface-tolerance\`: accept directly.
207	                - If any vertex exceeds \`REFIT_SKIP_MULTIPLIER * vertex_tolerance\` (currently 10.0×) or centroid exceeds \`REFIT_SKIP_MULTIPLIER * surface_tolerance\`: skip \`ni\` — too far for refitting to help.
208	                - Otherwise (between 1× and \`REFIT_SKIP_MULTIPLIER\`×): attempt a **re-fit** using all current trial faces plus \`ni\`. If the re-fit satisfies vertex tolerance and surface tolerance for **all** faces (existing and new), accept the re-fit. Otherwise, revert and skip \`ni\`.
209	            - If accepted: add \`ni\` to the trial's face list, add its vertices to the vertex set, push \`ni\` onto the BFS queue.
210	        - After BFS completes: do a final re-fit from all accumulated faces and vertices.
211	    c. **Acceptance tests** (per completed trial): call \`S::acceptance_test()\` for surface-type-specific post-BFS validation (e.g., angular coverage, solid-angle coverage, minimum face count). If validation fails, discard the trial.
212	    d. **Best candidate selection**: among all accepted trials for this starting face \`fi\`, keep the one with the largest total face area. Only two candidates need to be in memory at a time (current best and trial being evaluated).
213	    e. **Commit**: if a best candidate was found, create a hypothesis and assign it to all of its faces (mutating \`mesh.faces[].{type}_hypothesis\`). Faces assigned here will be skipped by subsequent starting faces.
214	3. After all starting faces have been processed: set any remaining \`UNDEDUCED\` faces to \`NO_HYPOTHESIS\`.
215	
216	**Curvature-guided seed ordering**: Start faces are processed in ascending order of a per-face *curvature-difference score* rather than mesh index order. The score is computed once per mesh (in stage 2, shared by all surface stages):
217	
218	1. **Per-face curvature summary**: for each mesh edge between adjacent faces, the discrete normal curvature along the crossing direction is κ ≈ θ/d (dihedral angle over centroid distance). A face's summary is the maximum κ over its edges — approximately its largest principal curvature.
219	2. **Neighbor difference**: a face's raw score is the maximum |summary difference| against its edge neighbors. Interior faces of any uniform surface (plane, cylinder, cone, sphere, torus) score ≈ 0 — curvature is locally constant even where it is nonzero — while faces at surface transitions score high. Crucially, this includes **tangent junctions** (fillet↔plane, fillet↔cylinder), which are G1-continuous and therefore invisible to dihedral-angle checks, but G2-discontinuous: curvature jumps from 0 to 1/r.
220	3. **Diffusion**: 3 Jacobi passes of neighbor averaging (new = ½·self + ½·mean(neighbors)) spread the junction signal a few hops, so ordering prefers deep-interior faces whose seed neighborhoods (1-ring / fan, radius ≈ 1–2) cannot touch a junction.
221	4. **Junction mask**: separately from the diffused ordering score, faces whose RAW score exceeds 50% of the local curvature magnitude (max of own and neighbors' summaries) are flagged as junction faces. The criterion is local-relative, not mesh-global: sharp creases dominate a global maximum and tangent junctions — the primary target — would fall below any fraction of it.
222	
223	Effect: interior seeds run first and commit large, correctly-fitted hypotheses; junction faces are usually absorbed by BFS growth before they ever become start faces (committed faces do not seed). **Seed gathering exclusion**: in pass 1, junction faces are also excluded from seed neighborhoods (conical fans, spherical 1-rings, toroidal hop-expansion), so seeds stay on one surface; a second pass retries remaining undeduced faces without the filter so surfaces composed entirely of transition faces (one-row fillets) still seed. Toroidal gathering hop-expands (up to 3 hops, targeting ≥ 16 faces) since small fillet tubes have little interior across the tube but extend along the fillet axis. Ordering and masks are deterministic: ties break by face index.
224	
225	**Relation to the older seeding masks (verified by measurement, 2026-07-15)**: the junction mask subsumed the \`multiface_planar\` mask (blob-shaped planar regions excluded from torus seeds) — full suite green and timings unchanged with it removed, so it was retired. It does NOT subsume the \`large_sphere\` mask: sphere interiors have uniform curvature and are never junction-flagged, and every adjacent quad-row band on a sphere lies exactly on some torus, so without the mask torus seeding/BFS tile large spheres with bogus-but-valid fits that only die in selection (correct output, catastrophic cost — pill_fine stage 2: 1.2 s → 429 s; rounded_cube_fine: 4.0 s → 479 s; full suite 3.3 → 18.5 min). The large-sphere mask stays.
226	
227	**Computational cost**: For each starting face, the number of seed trials is typically small (1 for planar/spherical/toroidal, 1–6 for conical/cylindrical). Each trial BFS covers the connected surface region. Since most faces on the same surface will discover the same region, the work is dominated by the first few faces that discover the region before their neighbors get committed. The curvature field costs O(N) plus three O(N) diffusion passes — negligible next to LM fitting.
228	
229	- With the \`--compare\` flag (applied to all surface types):
230	  - For each hypothesis, compute the centroid of each member face.
231	  - Project each centroid onto the fitted surface (nearest point on the surface).
232	  - Measure the distance from the projected point to the nearest surface in the reference STEP file using \`BRepExtrema_DistShapeShape\`.
233	  - If any projected centroid exceeds \`--surface-tolerance\`, report an error.
234	  - Note: centroid-to-surface distance measures both fitting accuracy and tessellation sagitta ($\approx h^2 / 8R$ for curvature radius $R$ and chord length $h$). For typical tessellations this is small relative to \`--surface-tolerance\`.
235	
236	#### 2.1 Deduce planar hypotheses
237	
238	Fit planar hypotheses to all connected sets of coplanar faces. Every face is assigned to exactly one planar hypothesis (including single-face hypotheses for faces on curved surfaces). A planar hypothesis consists of:
239	- \`normal: [f64; 3]\` — Unit normal vector pointing outward from the shell/solid.
240	- \`distance: f64\` — Signed distance from origin to plane along the normal (plane equation: normal · p = distance).
241	- \`error_max, error_min: f64\` — Maximum (positive) and minimum (most negative) signed distance from any vertex to the plane.
242	- \`error_abs_sum: f64\` — Sum of absolute vertex-to-plane distances.
243	
244	Runs the generic BFS hypothesis generation with the following parameterization:
245	
246	**Seed type**: The starting face \`fi\` alone. No additional seed faces are needed — a single face defines a plane.
247	
248	**Surface fit**: Area-weighted normal averaging. Each face's unit normal is weighted by the face's triangle area, then the weighted sum is normalized. The plane distance is the mean of \`normal · vertex\` over all vertices. This gives larger faces more influence on the plane orientation. See [SURFACE_FITTING.md](SURFACE_FITTING.md) for detailed fitting math.
249	
250	**Distance metric**: Signed distance from vertex to plane: \`d = normal · vertex - distance\`. All vertices must satisfy \`|d| ≤ vertex_tolerance\`.
251	
252	Because the vertex tolerance is tight for CAD meshes, the angular tolerance check is redundant for planar hypotheses — any face with all vertices in the tolerance band necessarily has a closely aligned normal. It is still applied for consistency with the generic algorithm.
253	
254	**Acceptance tests**: \`face_count >= 2\`. Single-face planes are not kept as hypotheses. Faces that end up in a single-face group are left as \`NO_HYPOTHESIS\`; stage 2.6 synthesizes single-face planar hypotheses for any faces that received no hypothesis from stages 2.1–2.5.
255	
256	**Execution order note**: Stages execute planar (2.1) → spherical (2.3) →
257	toroidal (2.5) → conical (2.2) → selection (2.6), keeping the historical
258	stage numbering for CLI/viz compatibility. The principle is most-constrained
259	surfaces first: every band of quad rows on a tessellated sphere lies exactly
260	on some cone or cylinder, and any adjacent pair of quad rows on a torus tube
261	likewise lies exactly on some cone — so if the conical stage ran first it
262	would tile spheres and fillets with bogus band hypotheses (slow, and they
263	starve the later stages' seeding). Sphere and torus acceptance tests are
264	selective enough to run early (solid-angle coverage; normal-based tube-span
265	coverage), and cylinders resist bogus torus fits cheaply (tube centers
266	collapse onto the axis; the (h,r) profile is a constant-radius line).
267	Conical seeding skips sphere- and torus-committed faces; conical BFS
268	*expansion* may still absorb them, so tangent junctions work.
269	
270	#### 2.2 Deduce conical/cylindrical hypotheses
271	
272	Fit conical and cylindrical hypotheses to connected sets of faces that lie on a common cone or cylinder. Cylinders and cones are unified into a single \`ConicalHypothesis\` struct (matching the C++ implementation), with \`half_angle == 0\` for cylinders. A hypothesis consists of:
273	- \`apex: [f64; 3]\` — The apex of the cone, or a point on the axis for cylinders.
274	- \`axis_direction: [f64; 3]\` — Unit direction vector along the axis.
275	- \`half_angle: f64\` — Half-angle of the cone in radians (0 for cylinders).
276	- \`radius_at_apex: f64\` — Cylinder radius when \`half_angle == 0\`. 0 for true cones.
277	- \`convex: bool\` — Whether face normals point away from the axis (convex, like the outside of a pipe) or toward it (concave, like the inside of a hole).
278	- \`error_max: f64\` — Maximum absolute distance from any vertex to the surface.
279	- \`centroid_error_max: f64\` — Maximum absolute distance from any face centroid to the surface.
280	- \`error_abs_sum: f64\` — Sum of absolute vertex-to-surface distances.
281	
282	The \`is_cylinder()\` method returns \`true\` when \`half_angle < 1e-4\`, used throughout to branch on cylinder vs. cone behavior.
283	
284	Runs the generic BFS hypothesis generation with the following parameterization:
285	
286	**Seed type**: Three faces \`(fi, f1, f2)\` — the starting face \`fi\`, a pairwise-qualified neighbor \`f1\` of \`fi\`, and another neighbor \`f2\` of \`fi\` where \`f2\` is NOT an edge-wise neighbor of \`f1\` (so \`fi\` is between \`f1\` and \`f2\` rather than at one end of a chain). Both \`f1\` and \`f2\` must be pairwise-qualified with \`fi\`. This places the seed faces at different circumferential positions around \`fi\`, providing angular diversity for the fit. Multiple seeds may be generated per starting face (one per valid \`(f1, f2)\` pair); the best trial by area is kept.
287	
288	*Pairwise qualification*: Two faces are pairwise qualified if they are neighbors, both unassigned for this surface type (\`conical_hypothesis < 0\`), have sufficiently different normals (cross product magnitude exceeds \`MIN_CROSS_THRESHOLD\`, e.g. 0.01), and the dihedral angle does not exceed \`--angular-tolerance\`. The cross product minimum is a seeding criterion only — during BFS expansion, adjacent faces on a finely tessellated cylinder legitimately have near-parallel normals.
289	
290	**Surface fit**: Attempts both a cone fit (5-parameter) and a cylinder fit (4-parameter) independently, then selects whichever achieves the lower maximum vertex-to-surface distance. If the cone's fitted half-angle is outside the valid range (2°–85°) or its apex is too far from the mesh, the cone fit is rejected. The fits use: (1) Axis direction from smallest eigenvector of the area-weighted normal covariance matrix $M = \sum w_i n_i n_i^T$. (2) For cylinders: project vertices perpendicular to the axis, fit a 2D circle for axis position and radius. For cones: estimate half-angle and apex from vertex positions along the axis. (3) LM refinement minimizing vertex-to-surface distances, with vertices **recentered** around their centroid for numerical stability (parameters shifted back after fitting), and \`patience=500\` to ensure convergence well below vertex tolerance. With \`--verbose 4\` or higher, each seed fit logs both the cone and cylinder fit attempts along with their max vertex/surface errors and rejection reasons. See [SURFACE_FITTING.md](SURFACE_FITTING.md) for detailed fitting math.
291	
292	**Distance metric**: For cylinders (axis origin \`o\`, axis direction \`a\`, radius \`r\`): distance from point \`p\` is $| \|p - o - ((p-o)\cdot a)a\| - r |$. For cones (apex, axis, half_angle): distance is the point-to-cone-surface distance along the radial direction.
293	
294	**BFS-specific checks**:
295	- **Convexity**: Compute the vector from the axis to the face's centroid. Check that the face's normal agrees with the hypothesis convexity (dot product with radial vector has the expected sign). Reject if inconsistent — this prevents merging the inner and outer surfaces of a thin-walled cylinder.
296	- **Normal-axis consistency (per face)**: on a cone, every facet normal satisfies |n·axis| = sin(half_angle) exactly (tessellation coarseness rotates facet normals *around* the axis, preserving the dot product). Candidate faces deviating more than 0.05 are rejected — faces on spheres and tori violate this as soon as BFS crosses tube rows, keeping doomed trials small.
297	
298	**Acceptance tests**:
299	- **Angular coverage validation**: (1) For each face, compute centroid angular position θ around the axis. (2) Sort θ values. (3) Find the largest gap and compute span = 2π − largest_gap. (4) The second-largest gap must be ≤ max(span / 3, \`max_gap\`), where \`max_gap\` is a coarse-tessellation floor. Ensures 3+ distinct angular clusters, preventing bogus hypotheses from non-cylindrical surface pairs. Also requires minimum face count. The \`max_gap\` floor (= \`angular_tol × angular_tol_multiplier\`, capped at span/2) is applied **only to cylinders** (half_angle ≈ 0) whose hypothesis **already spans ≥45°**: medium-resolution CAD steps small fillets into a few wide-gap rows (four rows across a 90° tube arc ⇒ 24° gaps > span/3 = 23°), and straight-edge fillets are cylinders. True cones keep the strict span/3 rule — a narrow corner sphere-blend patch fits a 6-face ~50° half-angle cone within tolerance and would otherwise win selection over the correct sphere. The span-≥45° gate keeps the floor from admitting thin cylinder bands of a smooth cone (a slice of a shallow cone is locally almost cylindrical); genuine coarse fillets span a real quarter/half-round (~70°+), spurious bands span ≤~15°.
300	- **Normal-axis consistency (aggregate)**: reject if the variance of n·axis over member faces exceeds 0.0005 — the faces lie on a sphere or torus, not a cone/cylinder. Without this check, stage 2.2 tiles torus fillets with bogus conical hypotheses, which starves torus seeding (stage 2.4 seeds exclude conical-committed faces).
301	
302	\`seed_vtol_multiplier\` is 1.0 for cones/cylinders (vertices lie precisely on the surface, not approximated). \`angular_tol_multiplier\` is 2.0: tessellated cones with N circumferential divisions have inter-strip dihedral angles of 360°/N, which can exceed the default angular tolerance; vertex-to-surface distance is the primary discriminator. The default \`--angular-tolerance\` is 25° so that seed-pair qualification (which uses the *raw*, unmultiplied tolerance) accepts the ~24° row steps of medium-resolution fillets.
303	
304	#### 2.3 Deduce spherical hypotheses
305	
306	Fit spherical hypotheses to connected sets of faces that lie on a common sphere. Faces with conical/cylindrical hypotheses are not excluded — a face can legitimately belong to both a cylinder and a sphere (e.g., equator faces). Stage 2.6 resolves overlapping hypotheses later. A spherical hypothesis consists of:
307	- \`center: [f64; 3]\` — Center of the sphere.
308	- \`radius: f64\` — Radius of the sphere (always positive).
309	- \`convex: bool\` — Whether face normals point away from the center (convex, like the outside of a ball) or toward it (concave, like a bowl).
310	- \`error_max: f64\` — Maximum absolute distance from any vertex to the sphere surface.
311	- \`error_abs_sum: f64\` — Sum of absolute vertex-to-surface distances.
312	
313	Runs the generic BFS hypothesis generation with the following parameterization:
314	
315	**Seed type**: The starting face \`fi\` plus all faces that share at least one vertex with \`fi\` (the **1-ring vertex neighborhood**). Only faces that are unassigned for this surface type are included. If fewer than 3 eligible faces remain in the seed, skip this starting face (not enough angular diversity for a reliable sphere fit). Returns a single seed per starting face.
316	
317	A vertex neighborhood on a sphere naturally provides normals spanning 3D, making it a good seed geometry for the 4-DOF sphere fit.
318	
319	**Surface fit**: Algebraic least-squares sphere fitting. Linearizes $|v - c|^2 = r^2$ as $2c_x x + 2c_y y + 2c_z z + k = x^2 + y^2 + z^2$ and solves via 4×4 normal equations with Gaussian elimination. Extracts center $(c_x, c_y, c_z)$ and radius $r = \sqrt{k + |c|^2}$. Optionally refined with Gauss-Newton minimizing vertex-to-sphere distances. Maximum radius bounded by \`bounding_box_diagonal * 1000\` as a numerical guard rail. See [SURFACE_FITTING.md](SURFACE_FITTING.md) for detailed fitting math.
320	
321	**Distance metric**: $| \|p - \text{center}\| - \text{radius} |$.
322	
323	**BFS-specific check — convexity**: Verify the face normal agrees with the hypothesis convexity (dot product of normal with vector from center to face centroid has the expected sign).
324	
325	**Acceptance tests — solid-angle coverage validation**: Computes area-weighted 3×3 covariance matrix of unit direction vectors from sphere center to face centroids. Checks eigenvalue ratio ($\lambda_3 / \lambda_1$) to ensure directions span 3D. Fillet faces along a cylinder produce $\lambda_3 \approx 0$ (1D strip), failing this test. Also requires minimum face count (\`MIN_SPHERE_FACES\`, at least 4 — a sphere needs at least 4 non-coplanar points for a unique fit).
326	
327	#### 2.4 Deduce toroidal hypotheses
328	
329	Fit toroidal hypotheses to connected sets of faces that lie on a common torus. Toroidal surfaces commonly appear as fillets, blends, and pipe elbows. A toroidal hypothesis consists of:
330	- \`center: [f64; 3]\` — Center of the torus (on the axis, in the plane of the major circle).
331	- \`axis_direction: [f64; 3]\` — Unit direction vector along the torus axis.
332	- \`major_radius: f64\` — Distance from center to the tube centerline ($R$).
333	- \`minor_radius: f64\` — Radius of the tube cross-section ($r$).
334	- \`convex: bool\` — Whether the torus patch is on the outer (convex) or inner (concave) surface of the tube.
335	- \`error_max: f64\` — Maximum absolute distance from any vertex to the torus surface.
336	- \`error_abs_sum: f64\` — Sum of absolute vertex-to-surface distances.
337	
338	Runs the generic BFS hypothesis generation with the following parameterization:
339	
340	**Seed type**: Two seed sources:
341	- *1-ring seeds* (same as spherical): the starting face \`fi\` plus the 1-ring vertex neighborhood, for faces not committed to any other hypothesis type.
342	- *Strip-merge seeds*: for faces committed to a cylindrical hypothesis, a 3-ring edge neighborhood — but only when it spans ≥ 2 distinct conical hypotheses. A coarsely tessellated torus (e.g. a pipe elbow) gets tiled by narrow cylinder strips in stage 2.2, each spanning too little of the major arc for a 1-ring torus fit; a 3-ring neighborhood spans several strips and provides the major-arc curvature. A plain cylinder region is a single hypothesis and is skipped cheaply.
343	
344	Faces already assigned to spherical hypotheses are excluded from seeds. Seeds with fewer than \`MIN_TORUS_FACES\` (7) faces or fewer than 9 unique vertices are skipped (torus has 7 DOF, needs sufficient data for a reliable fit). Returns a single seed per starting face.
345	
346	**Surface fit**: Tries **two methods** and keeps whichever achieves the lowest maximum vertex-to-torus distance: (1) **Medial axis / tube center method**: estimates minor radius from normal-line intersections of adjacent face pairs, computes tube centers $k_i = p_i \pm r \cdot n_i$, fits a 3D circle to the tube centers for axis direction and major radius, then LM refinement of 7 parameters $[C_x, C_y, C_z, \alpha, \beta, R, r]$. (2) **SOR axis method**: estimates axis from PCA of vertex positions (smallest eigenvector), center via Kasa circle fit, radii from (h,r) profile circle fit, then same LM refinement. Rejects if minor_radius ≤ 0, major_radius ≤ 0, or minor_radius > major_radius (self-intersecting). See [SURFACE_FITTING.md](SURFACE_FITTING.md) for detailed fitting math.
347	
348	**Distance metric**: $|\sqrt{(\text{radial} - R)^2 + \text{axial}^2} - r|$ where $\text{axial} = (p - C) \cdot \hat{a}$ and $\text{radial} = |(p - C) - \text{axial} \cdot \hat{a}|$.
349	
350	**BFS-specific check — convexity**: Compute the vector from the nearest tube center point to the face centroid. The tube center is $c = C + R \cdot \hat{r}$ where $\hat{r}$ is the radial unit vector from the torus axis to the point. Check that the face normal's dot product with this vector has the expected sign.
351	
352	**Acceptance tests**: (1) Minimum \`MIN_TORUS_FACES\` (7) — torus has 7 DOF. (2) **Angular coverage around the major circle** (same azimuthal algorithm as cylindrical/conical). (3) **Minor circle coverage from face normals**: the tube angle of each face is measured from its *normal* (ψ = atan2(n·â, n·r̂)); the span (largest-gap algorithm) must be ≥ 0.3 rad. Normals — not centroid positions — are the decisive signal: a flat annulus or a cylinder band lies exactly on a one-parameter family of degenerate tori and can span a nonzero *positional* tube arc on a large-tube-radius member of that family, but its normals are all parallel (tube span ≈ 0).
353	
354	\`angular_tol_multiplier\` is 2.0: torus quads twist (doubly curved), so stage 1.3 coplanar fusion often fails on fillet patches, leaving raw triangles whose normals differ by up to ~2× the tessellation angular step.
355	
356	#### 2.5 Select surfaces to use for reconstruction
357	Assign each mesh face to exactly one "selected surface" for reconstruction. Since stages 2.1–2.4 produce hypotheses that may overlap (a face can belong to one planar, one conical/cylindrical, one spherical, and one toroidal hypothesis simultaneously), surface selection must resolve these overlaps.
358	
359	
360	**Greedy area-based selection:**
361	
362	The algorithm selects hypotheses greedily by total mesh face area, largest first. This naturally resolves conflicts: real surface hypotheses cover many faces with large total area, while bogus hypotheses (e.g., a near-degenerate r=139mm sphere from 3 nearly-coplanar faces) cover minimal area and lose their faces to correctly-fitted surfaces.
363	
364	**Algorithm:**
365	1. Compute the geometric area of each mesh face.
366	2. Build a candidate list of all hypotheses (planar with face count > 1, conical/cylindrical, spherical, toroidal). Each candidate tracks its set of member faces and their total area.
367	
368	3. **Greedy loop**: while unassigned faces remain:
369	   a. For each candidate, compute the total area of its still-unassigned faces.
370	   b. Select the candidate with the largest remaining area.
371	   c. Assign all its still-unassigned faces to it as a selected surface.
372	   d. Remove those faces from all other candidates' pools.
373	4. Assign any remaining unassigned faces to their single-face planar hypothesis (fallback for faces on surfaces not yet fitted: ruled, freeform). Stage 2.7 may replace some of these with NURBS surfaces.
374	
375	5. Build a \`Vec<SelectedSurface>\`, one per selected hypothesis, containing:
376	   - \`surface_type: SurfaceType\` — enum: Planar, Cylindrical, Spherical, Conical, Toroidal, BSpline.
377	
378	   - \`hypothesis_index: usize\` — index into the appropriate hypothesis vector in Stage2Output.
379	   - \`faces: Vec<usize>\` — mesh face indices assigned to this surface.
380	   - \`vertices: Vec<usize>\` — mesh vertex indices on this surface (union of face vertices).
381	6. **Absorb redundant surfaces** (\`absorb_redundant_surfaces\`; CLI sub-stage **2.8**, skippable via \`--skip-stage=2.8\`, e.g. for exact planar-only conversion where the chordal slop of the loose rule would put absorbed facets up to \`--surface-tolerance\` off their assigned surface): merge a selected surface S into an adjacent, larger-area surface T when all of S's vertices lie within tolerance of T's fitted geometry AND all of S's face centroids lie within \`--surface-tolerance\` of T. Two rules:
382	   - *Tight rule* (10× vertex tolerance — exact overlap). The load-bearing rule, verified per-merge across the corpus 2026-07-17. Merges **spurious sub-fits** — a narrow band of faces on a cone/sphere fits an "exact" cylinder (nosecone: a 6-face r=12.4992 cylinder band on the r=12.5 sphere) and gets committed before the real surface's BFS reaches those faces — and **split fragments of one real surface** (nosecone_truncated: a 31-face piece of the real cone, identical apex/half-angle to the 117-face main hypothesis, rejoined here). Left unmerged these punch fake edges/vertices into the region and break stage 3 (nosecone_truncated: fake corner vertex 80.6 mm off the model, stage 3.1 \`--compare\` hard failure).
383	   - *Loose rule* (\`--surface-tolerance\`, only for S with ≤ 2 faces into T with > 2 faces). **Its original motivation ("tangent-junction bridge facets" — a facet straddling a tangent junction with vertices on both surfaces) was tested 2026-07-17 and is false for the current corpus**: CAD tessellators emit seam edges along B-rep face boundaries even where surfaces are tangent (nosecone cone–sphere tangency: 0 straddling STL triangles; 104 vertices exactly on the tangency ring). What it actually fires on today (stepped_block_toroidal: 2 merges) is *real* narrow planar faces tessellated as a single rectangle (one fused quad — below the ≥ 2-face planar hypothesis minimum, so they arrive as single-face orphans), replacing them with a neighbor's geometry 0.12–0.27 mm off; in planar-only mode it merged ~128k exact facet planes on 3DBenchy with up to 0.4 mm slop (794 BRepCheck face failures). Deletion/tightening candidate (see TODO.md); the underlying gap is that a single-fused-quad real face cannot form a planar hypothesis (before stage 1.3 fusion it was two coplanar triangles, which would qualify).
384	   The centroid check prevents absorbing flat end caps triangulated as a fan from a boundary vertex (all cap *vertices* lie on the adjacent cone/cylinder's boundary circle, but face interiors do not).
385	7. **Prune unselected hypotheses**: remove any hypothesis that was not selected from the hypothesis vectors in \`Stage2Output\`. Remap \`SelectedSurface\` indices to match the compacted vectors. After this step, \`Stage2Output.conical_hypotheses\` (etc.) contain only selected hypotheses.
386	8. Validate: every face is assigned to exactly one selected surface.
387	9. Print a summary: count of selected surfaces by type, total faces covered.
388	
389	**Why this works better than a fixed type-priority rule:** A fixed priority (e.g., spherical > cylindrical > multi-face planar) fails when a bogus hypothesis of a high-priority type (3-face r=139mm sphere) competes with a correct hypothesis of a lower-priority type (35-face r=2mm cylinder). Area-based greedy selection naturally picks the correct hypothesis because it covers vastly more area.
390	
391	**Nerfed tests:**
392	- (none currently — \`onshape_pipe_elbow_stage26\` now passes \`--compare\`, see note below.)
393	- \`onshape_pipe_elbow_stage26_compare\`: Passes \`--compare\` with 290 cylindrical + 0 toroidal selections. The pipe elbow's torus surface is tessellated into many narrow strips whose normals span < 180°, so stage 2.5 torus fitting doesn't fire (normals look locally cylindrical). This is expected — the torus is decomposed into cylindrical patches that individually pass \`--compare\`.
394	
395	- With the \`--compare\` flag, for each selected surface:
396	    - Compute face centroids and project them onto the selected surface.
397	    - Measure distance from each projected centroid to the nearest surface in the reference STEP file.
398	    - For all hypotheses, report an error if distance exceeds \`--surface-tolerance\`.
399	
400	#### 2.6 Surface refitting (boundary refinement — implemented; CLI sub-stage **2.9**, skippable via \`--skip-stage=2.9\`)
401	
402	After selection and absorption, some border faces may have been absorbed by a hypothesis that fits within tolerance but isn't the best fit; the misassignment shifts the mesh region boundary by a row, which shifts reconstructed edge boundary vertices and corner vertices off the true feature curves.
403	
404	**Algorithm (\`refine_boundary_assignments\`, as implemented):**
405	1. Build the face → selected-surface map; walk every face that has a neighbor in a *different* selected surface (region-boundary faces — the original plan keyed on multi-hypothesis membership, but pruning/absorption destroy that; adjacency is the modern equivalent).
406	2. Compute the face's max vertex distance to its own surface and to each adjacent selected surface.
```
</details>

```
82	  vertex edges (MakeEdge \`DifferentPointsOnClosedCurve\`), and 684 of 68924
83	  edge curves come from mesh-boundary-chain fits because surface-surface
84	  intersection failed.
85	
86	- 3DBenchy planar-only mode (\`--skip-stage=2.2,2.3,2.5,2.7\`) now runs
87	  END-TO-END (~5 min, 704MB STEP, 1 solid, volume 15393.7mm³ ≈ mesh volume;
88	  all 196393 mesh faces covered by 41324 planar faces, 41324/41324 OCCT faces
89	  created), after three fixes landed 2026-07-16 (uncommitted; suite 494 green
90	  + clippy clean after each): (1) \`compute_edge_curve\` (src/stage3.rs ~1577)
91	  guarded against a degenerate trim span — \`Geom_TrimmedCurve\` threw
92	  \`U1 == U2\` when both edge endpoints projected to the same curve parameter;
93	  now Errs into the mesh-boundary-chain fallback, matching
94	  \`candidate_trim_parameters\`. (2) \`refine_corner_vertices\` (src/stage3.rs
95	  ~712) rejects a refined corner position landing within
96	  max(vertex_tolerance, 1e-6mm) of an edge-adjacent corner's current
97	  position (vertex keeps the raw mesh position) — cyclic projection had
98	  pulled BOTH endpoints of edge 5715 onto the same near-parallel-plane
99	  intersection fixpoint (4.25e-14mm apart), collapsing the edge so MakeEdge
100	  failed \`DifferentPointsOnClosedCurve\` and even its straight-line fallback
101	  failed. NOT a mesh-weld gap: weld is grid+27-neighbor tolerance-based and
102	  repair creates no vertices, so distinct mesh vertices are always ≥ weld
103	  tolerance apart; only refinement can create sub-tolerance pairs. (3)
104	  \`build_pcurve\` (src/stage3.rs ~5454) dedupes UV interpolation samples and
105	  emits a degree-1 (possibly constant) 2-pole B-spline pcurve when an edge's
106	  projection onto a face's chart is pointlike — edge 25813 runs purely along
107	  face 7145's plane normal (corner refinement legitimately offset both
108	  endpoints ~0.02mm along x within the 2×surface_tolerance contract), so all
109	  UV samples coincided and \`Geom2dAPI_Interpolate\`'s ctor threw
110	  \`Standard_ConstructionError\` (it rejects consecutive points closer than
111	  its 1e-10 tolerance); the edge's 3D extent goes into the returned
112	  deviation → edge tolerance. NOTE these three fixes harden shared stage-3
113	  paths, so they may also help the normal curved-fit pipeline's stage 3.4+
114	  (untested here; its known blocker — face 5841, 3 winding loops — is the
115	  separate item above).
116	
117	- **Planar-only geometry errors ROOT-CAUSED to the absorb pass (2026-07-16
118	  follow-up session; absorb/refine now numbered + skippable).** With absorb
119	  on, 794/41324 faces + the solid failed BRepCheck; the absorb pass's loose
120	  ≤2-face-orphan rule merges facets into neighbors within
121	  \`--surface-tolerance\` (0.4mm) chordal slop, and on benchy it merged ~128k
122	  of 169,890 facet surfaces — each absorbed facet then claims to lie on a
123	  plane up to 0.4mm away (corner refinement compounds this, moving vertices
124	  up to 2× surface tolerance onto sloppy-plane intersections). Change:
125	  absorb and boundary refinement are now CLI sub-stages **2.8** and **2.9**
126	  (\`Stage::last_minor(2)\` = 9; \`--stage=2.7\` now stops before them; the
127	  \`--stage=2.6\`/\`2.7\` integration-test helpers migrated to \`--stage=2\`,
128	  preserving their post-absorb assertions; suite 494 green, clippy clean).
129	  Exact planar-only mode is \`--skip-stage=2.2,2.3,2.5,2.7,2.8\`: BRepCheck
130	  face failures 794 → **4** (0.002%), the SOLID passes BRepCheck, and
131	  volume = 15550.53 mm³ = the official 3DBenchy 15.55 cm³ (absorb-on had
132	  lost 1% to slop: 15393.7). Cost: 169,890 faces, 2.3GB STEP, ~10 min.
133	  Stage 2.9 is harmless in this mode (196 tolerance-level moves;
134	  corner-refinement guard fired 0 times). Residual: the 4 remaining
135	  BRepCheck failures are NOT absorb-related — face 40 (planar, 13 edges,
136	  TWO wires, 1.32mm²) fails "invalid imbrication of wires / unorientable"
137	  (the multi-loop / disconnected-region family, cf. face 5841), and faces
138	  19767/160616/163493 are micro-slivers (0.0004–0.0017 mm², 4 edges) with
139	  "wire 0 invalid" — likely micro-edge / tolerance-stretched wire
140	  construction.
141	
142	# Deferred (until a model needs it)
143	
144	- Boundary crack stitching (mesh repair): close hairline cracks by pairing
145	  nearly-coincident open boundary edges and re-welding their endpoints at a
146	  progressively larger tolerance (bounded by --vertex-tolerance) — what
147	  netfabb "stitch triangles" and CGAL \`stitch_borders\` do. Common in CAD
148	  exports where facets along a shared edge disagree at ~1e-6. No current
```

```
1	# Brepper - STL to STEP Converter
2	
3	Convert triangulated STL meshes from CAD exports into parametric STEP files with fitted analytic and freeform surfaces.
4	
5	## Usage
6	
7	\`\`\`
8	brepper - Convert STL mesh to STEP with fitted surfaces
9	
10	USAGE:
11	    brepper [OPTIONS] <input.stl> [-o <output.step>]
12	
13	REQUIRED:
14	    <input.stl>              Input STL file (binary or ASCII)
15	
16	GENERAL OPTIONS:
17	    -o <step>, --output=<step>  Output STEP file
18	    --stl-units=<units>      Units used by the STL file (mm, cm, m, in, ft, um; default: mm)
19	    --step-units=<units>     Units to use in exported STEP file (default: mm)
20	    --compare=<step>         STEP file to compare to at each step
21	    --vertex-tolerance=<value>  Fitting tolerance in STL units (default: 5e-5)
22	    --surface-tolerance=<value> Surface-to-face offset tolerance in STL units (default: 0.4)
23	    --angular-tolerance=<deg>   Max dihedral angle between adjacent triangles on the same surface (default: 25)
24	    -v, --verbose[=LEVEL]    Verbosity level: 1 = stage summaries, 2 = per-hypothesis details,
25	                             3 = commit details/stats/profiles, 4 = per-seed details, 5 = BFS face trace
26	    -q, --quiet              Suppress non-error output
27	    --strict-mesh            Fail on mesh defects instead of repairing them (dropping
28	                             duplicate-vertex and coincident faces, stitching T-junction
29	                             slivers, unifying winding, reorienting inverted shells)
30	    --debug                  Enable debug output and intermediate files
31	    --stage=<stage>          Stop after stage, e.g. 2.2 (default: 4.1). --stage=2 stops after all of stage2
32	    --skip-stage=<stages>    Skip sub-stages entirely while still running the rest of the pipeline
33	                             to output (comma-separated; 2.2 conical, 2.3 spherical, 2.5 toroidal,
34	                             2.7 NURBS, 2.8 redundant-surface absorption, 2.9 boundary refinement).
35	                             --skip-stage=2.2,2.3,2.5,2.7,2.8 is exact planar-only mode
36	    --viz-stages=<stages>    Open interactive 3D visualization at specified stages (e.g. 2.1,2.2,2.3)
37	    --viz-stages-level=<N>   Viz detail level: 1 = committed hypotheses (default), 2 = seeds, 3 = BFS faces
38	\`\`\`
39	
40	### Examples
41	
42	\`\`\`bash
43	# Full conversion
44	./target/debug/brepper input.stl -o output.step
45	
46	# Just load and validate mesh (stage 1.2)
47	./target/debug/brepper input.stl --stage 1.2 -v
48	
49	# Compare mesh against reference STEP at each stage
50	./target/debug/brepper input.stl --compare reference.step --stage 1 -v
51	\`\`\`
52	
53	See [DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md](DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md) for full documentation.
54	
55	### Tolerance Settings
56	
57	#### Surface Tolerance
58	
59	This is how far the surface of a mesh triangle is allowed to deviate from the surface it is approximating.
60	
61	Onshape calls this "Chordal tolerance (mm)", and the preset values are:
62	- Coarse: 0.24 mm
63	- Medium: 0.12 mm
64	- Fine: 0.06 mm
65	
66	Fusion 360 calls this "Surface Deviation", and the preset values seem to vary based on model size:
67	- Low: 1x
68	- Medium: 2.5x
69	- High: 8x
70	
71	#### Angular Tolerance
72	
73	This is the maximum angle between adjacent triangle faces that approximate a smooth surface.
74	
75	Onshape calls this "Angular deviation (deg)", and the present values are:
76	- Coarse: 12.5 deg
77	- Medium: 6.25 deg
78	- Fine: 2.5 deg
79	
80	Fusion 360 calls this "Normal Deviation", and the preset values are:
81	- Low: 30.0
82	- Medium: 15.0
83	- High: 10.0
84	In theory "Normal Deviation" should be the maximum angular error of the face relative to the surface normal, but in practice it seems to result in the given maximum angular deviation.
85	
86	### OCCT-Generated Test Models
87	
88	Models in \`tests/occt/\` and \`tests/nurbs/\` are generated directly via OCCT by
89	the \`gen-test-models\` binary (\`./scripts/generate_occt_models.sh\`) — used for
90	models that ccad cannot express. \`tests/occt/\` holds analytic models such as
91	\`hollow_cube\` (a solid with a fully enclosed internal void, for multi-shell
92	solid support). \`tests/nurbs/\` holds freeform models (exact B-spline patches,
93	conic/B-spline extrusions) exercising the stage 2.7 NURBS fallback; all 8
94	pass full \`--compare\` — see \`tests/nurbs/README.md\`. The generated STL/STEP pairs are committed.
95	
96	### Generating Test Models
97	
98	Some tests use models generated via [CodeCAD](https://codecad.xyz/). The generated STL/STEP files are committed to the repo, so CodeCAD is not required to run tests.
99	
100	To add or modify these models:
101	1. Install CodeCAD
102	2. Edit files in \`tests/ccad/parts/\` — see \`tests/ccad/README.md\` for primitive conventions and placement rules
103	3. Run \`./tests/ccad/generate_models.sh\`
104	
105	**Important:** \`box(w,d,h)\` places a corner at the origin, not centered. Use \`center_xyz()\` to center. Each part file includes analytical surface area and volume in its header comments.
106	
107	### Interactive Debugging Visualization
108	
109	Use \`--viz-stages\` to open a 3D window and step through BFS hypothesis deduction interactively.
110	Use \`--viz-stages-level\` to control how often the visualization pauses:
111	- Level 1 (default): Pauses at each committed hypothesis
112	- Level 2: Also pauses at each accepted seed before BFS expansion
113	- Level 3: Also pauses at each face accepted during BFS expansion
114	
115	\`\`\`bash
116	# Visualize planar and cylindrical hypothesis stages (committed hypotheses only)
117	brepper input.stl --viz-stages=2.1,2.2
118	
119	# Visualize with seed-level detail
120	brepper input.stl --viz-stages=2.2 --viz-stages-level=2
121	
122	# Visualize all stage 2 hypothesis types with STEP comparison overlay
123	brepper input.stl --compare reference.step --viz-stages=2.1,2.2,2.3
124	\`\`\`
125	
126	At each BFS step, the window shows the STL mesh with highlighted faces: seed faces in green, previously accepted faces in blue, the just-accepted face in dark blue, and BFS queue frontier edges in red. Cylindrical and spherical hypotheses also display translucent geometry overlays. When \`--compare\` is provided, the STEP surface is shown at 35% opacity for reference.
127	
128	| Action | Control |
129	|--------|:--------|
130	| Next step | \`Space\` |
131	| Skip to next seed/hypothesis | \`Shift+Space\` |
132	| Skip to face seed | \`Click\` on a face |
133	| Quit visualization | \`Q\` |
134	
135	Supported stages: \`2.1\` (planar), \`2.2\` (cylindrical), \`2.3\` (spherical), \`2.4\` (conical), \`2.6\` (surface selection), \`2.7\` (NURBS fallback), \`3.3\` (edge curves), \`3.4\` (faces), \`3.5\` (shells), \`3.6\` (solids).
136	
137	
138	## Viewer
139	
140	\`viewer\` is an interactive 3D viewer for STL and STEP files.
141	
142	\`\`\`
143	USAGE:
144	    viewer [OPTIONS] <file>...
145	
146	ARGUMENTS:
147	    <file>...   One or more STL or STEP files to view (detected by extension)
148	
149	OPTIONS:
150	    --deflection <value>          Linear deflection for STEP tessellation (default: 0.1)
151	    --angular-deflection <value>  Angular deflection for STEP tessellation in degrees (default: 28.6)
152	\`\`\`
153	
154	Multiple files can be loaded simultaneously; each is rendered in a distinct color.
155	
156	### Examples
157	
158	\`\`\`bash
159	# View a single STEP file
160	./target/release/viewer part.step
161	
162	# View a STEP and an STL side by side
163	./target/release/viewer reference.step output.stl
164	
165	# Finer tessellation for curved surfaces
166	./target/release/viewer part.step --deflection 0.01 --angular-deflection 5.7
167	\`\`\`
168	
169	### Controls
170	
171	| Action | Control |
172	|--------|:--------|
173	| Rotate (around model center) | Left-click + drag |
174	| Pan | Right-click + drag |
175	| Zoom (at cursor) | Scroll wheel |
176	| Quit | \`q\` or \`Esc\` |
177	| Toggle perspective/orthographic | \`p\` |
178	| Toggle wireframe edges | \`e\` |
179	| Toggle soft (mesh) edges | \`Shift+E\` |
180	| Toggle solid faces | \`s\` |
181	| Toggle hidden-edge removal | \`h\` |
182	
183	Rendering uses PBR shading with ambient and two directional lights. Sharp feature edges (boundary edges and edges where the dihedral angle exceeds ~45°) are drawn as a solid wireframe overlay. Softer mesh edges (dihedral angle below ~45°) are drawn at 50% opacity and can be toggled independently with \`Shift+E\`. Zooming targets the point under the mouse cursor.
184

186

204	- Surface selection (stage 2.6) produces correct surface type counts for known models (block_with_hole=6 planar+1 cylindrical, pipe=2 planar+2 cylindrical, ball_on_cylinder=1 planar+1 cylindrical+1 spherical, hemisphere=1 planar+1 spherical, simple_sphere=1 spherical, cone_15x20=1 planar+1 conical, simple_cone=2 planar+1 conical)
```
