Full 477-test suite green.
```
431	
432	**Periodic closed-band design — as built (2026-07-15; probed conventions in \`tmp/probe_periodic.rs\`)**: the seam-split's planned replacement is implemented; the deleted stage 3.1 compare exemption (plus \`shared_surface_group\`/\`is_seam_cut_pair\`) was the acceptance test. What a maintainer needs to know:
433	- *OCCT periodic-fit conventions (probed, and confirmed in the \`GeomAPI_PointsToBSplineSurface.cxx\` source)*: periodic along U = the array's FIRST index; supply each row once (OCCT appends the wrap-around copy of row 1 itself; a duplicated closing row would make the system singular); the result already has \`SetUPeriodic()\` applied. Closure is C1, not C2 (D2U jumps at the wrap; periodic interpolation constrains tangents only). Chord-length parameters start at the first supplied row, so U=0 is exactly the cut path — the seam gauge needs no search, sidestepping \`plan_seam_anchors\`' vertex-pair compatibility error (the strip mesh's rings are azimuthally misaligned by ~half a step, so matching vertex pairs do not exist).
434	- *The around-the-band axis is NOT always U in the Floater square* (elliptical_cylinder's cut copies land as the u-extreme sides, making v the around axis): \`cut_around_axis\` detects it from the \`CUT_OUTSIDE\` loop entries, and \`fit_grid\` transposes the array so the surface's U is always the around axis — \`BSplineHypothesis.u_periodic\` is a single flag, no per-axis plumbing. Transposition negates d1u×d1v, so the outward-orientation flip reverses the second index and XORs with the transpose.
435	- *2π normalization*: the stage-3.4 wrapped-face machinery (branch unwrap in \`sample_edge_uv\`, winding classification, \`align_loop\` offsets, pcurve shifts, seam handling — \`grep TWO_PI src/stage3.rs\`) hard-codes the period; \`SetUKnots\` rescales the fitted knots [0,1]→[0,2π] exactly, after which it all applies verbatim once the chart reports \`u_periodic()\`. The chart returns principal U in [0,2π) (ShapeAnalysis does this on periodic surfaces; the chart adds a \`rem_euclid\` guard) and skips the \`home\`-anchor U-branch resolution, which remains for the seam-split fallback's clamped self-touching surface.
436	- *Junction curves*: see "Periodic-band junctions (stage 3.3)" above — closed-loop edges need periodic curves for seam re-anchoring; both the GeomConvert periodicization and the periodic chain-repair arm were required on the first model.
437	- *Debug probes*: \`BREPPER_DEBUG_EDGE_UV=1\` prints every chart projection during edge sampling; \`--stage=3.4 -v\` prints per-edge pcurve deviations + UV endpoints per face; the \`-vvv\` fit-residual-vs-own-grid probe separates fit error from sampler/projection error.
438	
439	**Implementation notes for the NURBS pass** (knowledge from the stage-3 rework, 2026-07-08; all items below are now implemented as described — kept for reference):
440	
441	- *The chart contract.* A chart provides \`point_uv(p) → (u, v, pole_radius)\` and \`point_at(u, v)\`, plus \`u_periodic()\`/\`v_periodic()\`. For a B-spline chart: \`pole_radius\` is ∞ (like the plane chart — fitted height-field patches have no UV degeneracies), and **both periodic flags must be false for open patches** (\`u_periodic()\` is true only for the periodic closed-band fit, whose knots are rescaled to the 2π period the machinery assumes). Every mod-2π unwrap/branch-lift in stage 3.4 is gated on those flags; reducing non-periodic parameter coordinates mod 2π silently corrupts them (this was a real bug on plane charts, masked for months by adaptive densification — see LEARNINGS). With both flags false, the whole seam/winding/degenerate-pole machinery self-disables and B-spline faces take the plain-loop path end to end.
442	- *The chart must wrap the exact \`Handle(Geom_BSplineSurface)\` stored in \`FaceDescriptor.surface\`* — never a reconstruction. (Analytic charts get away with rebuilding the gp_Ax3 only because that construction is deterministic.) Use one \`ShapeAnalysis_Surface\` per face: \`new_handlegeomsurface\` + \`value_of_uv(&mut self, pnt, precision)\` — note \`&mut\` (it caches internally). For sampling along an edge curve, prefer \`next_value_of_uv(prev_uv, pnt, preci, maxpreci)\` — it seeds the search from the previous sample, the B-spline analog of the analytic charts' branch-continuity unwrap. Validate each solution with \`gap()\` (ValueOfUV can return a poor projection rather than failing) and fall back to a fresh \`value_of_uv\` when the gap exceeds tolerance.
443	- *Every \`match\` on \`SelectedSurface\` needs a \`BSpline\` arm.* From crawling all of them: \`create_occt_surface\` and \`surface_faces\` (3.1), \`surface_normal_at_point\` (3.2 tangency detection and the 3.4 orientation compare — use D1 cross product on the handle), \`project_point_to_selected\` (corner-vertex refinement), \`build_face_chart\`, the concave-flag matches in \`assemble_face\`/\`create_occt_faces_all\`, the tangent-edge dispatch in 3.3, and the cosmetic \`stype\` labels + viz overlays.
444	- *Fix the fitted patch's normal orientation at fit time.* The shell orientation of a face comes from a single \`concave\` flag (natural surface normal vs. material). \`GeomAPI_PointsToBSplineSurface\` follows the orientation of the input point grid, so build the grid such that d1u×d1v matches the mesh's outward normals — then B-spline faces are always \`concave = false\`/FORWARD and tangency-detection normals need no sign logic. Choosing the convention at fit time is one line; recovering it later is a per-face heuristic.
445	- *Edge curves will lean on the boundary chain.* Expect \`GeomAPI_IntSS\` to be flakier against B-spline surfaces than between quadrics, and expect tangent-classified junctions (a fitted patch usually meets its neighbors near-tangentially) to have no analytic construction — both routes end at the mesh-boundary-chain machinery (\`repair_edge_curve_from_boundary\`, \`edge_curve_from_boundary_chain\`), which is surface-pair-agnostic. The B-spline third arm is implemented: the repair now fits line, circle, AND a \`GeomAPI_PointsToBSpline\` curve through the chain vertices (open chains), keeping whichever deviates least from the chain. The first model to demand it was elliptical_arch — one of its two arch∩wall ellipse junctions came back from IntSS degenerate, and the circle repair's 1 mm deviation cost 1.6% of the solid volume; the chain's B-spline curve fits it to 1.8e-2 mm.
446	- *What NURBS will not stress — don't build for it:* seams, winding loops, degenerate pole edges, seam-anchor planning, and edge splitting all apply only to periodic/wrapped faces; non-periodic B-spline patches never take those paths.
447	- *Bindings verified present (2026-07-08):* \`geom_api::PointsToBSplineSurface\` (grid constructor \`new_array2ofpnt_int2_shape_real\`), \`geom_api::PointsToBSpline\` (curve fit), \`shape_analysis::Surface::{new_handlegeomsurface, value_of_uv, next_value_of_uv, gap}\`, \`geom::BSplineSurface\`; the generic \`geom::Surface::{u_iso, v_iso, bounds, d0, d1}\` all work on B-splines.
448	- *Test models: DONE (2026-07-09), in \`tests/nurbs/\` — 8 STL/STEP pairs from \`gen-test-models\` (src/bin/gen_test_models.rs); see tests/nurbs/README.md for the difficulty ladder.* The original sketch (fit reference STEPs with \`PointsToBSplineSurface\` from sampled height-field grids) was rejected: the recognizer itself will use OCCT's fitters, so fitted references are circular (shared biases cancel) and only approximate. Instead every reference is EXACT — explicit-pole \`Geom_BSplineSurface\` lattices, conic/B-spline extrusions (\`Geom_SurfaceOfLinearExtrusion\`), and booleans of those. The doubly-curved patch models ship scripted regular-grid STLs sampled exactly on the reference surface (vertices on-surface to f32 precision); the ruled/extruded models (elliptical_arch, elliptical_cylinder, bspline_wave) deliberately ship as STRIP meshes — full-length strips with NO interior vertices along the ruling, exactly what real CAD exporters emit for extrusions — so the fitter must be designed for 2-ring sampling along a ruling (e.g. a ruled / low-v-degree fit) rather than assuming grid-like point coverage. The conic models' strips come from genuine OCCT tessellation; the wave's are scripted, because BRepMesh internally grid-refines B-spline-based surfaces at any deflection/angle setting (conic extrusions it strips naturally). The \`--compare\` gates are hard errors (volume >1%, round-trip re-read, surface distance), so NURBS reconstruction gets real pass/fail coverage from the first model. Generator-side caveat worth remembering: neither BRepGProp quadrature is trustworthy alone on freeform faces — fixed-order Gauss is ~0.5% low on localized bicubic bumps and ~4e-5 off on B-spline extrusions, while the adaptive variant measured 4% HIGH on a boolean-trimmed extrusion sheet and 0.3% LOW on the same surface as a natural prism face; the generator validates B-rep volumes against the closed STL meshes (scripted ones directly, tessellated ones parsed back) and against machine-exact profile integrals where available.
449	
450	---
451	
452	### Algorithm Reference
453	
454	Surface fitting math details (plane fitting, cylinder fitting, sphere fitting, cone fitting, torus fitting) and algorithmic techniques (Unified SOR Framework, Gaussian Map Classification, Medial Axis / Tube Center Method, Quadric Fitting) are documented in [SURFACE_FITTING.md](SURFACE_FITTING.md).
455	
456	---
457	
458	### Stage 3: Surface Reconstruction
459	
460	Build OCCT B-Rep topology (faces, edges, vertices) from the selected surfaces. This is the most complex stage — surface intersections in 3D are numerically delicate, and small perturbations can cause large changes in intersection curves or cause them to disappear.
461	
462	**Core data structures:**
463	
464	A vector of \`FaceDescriptor\` structs, one per selected surface:
465	- \`selected_surface_idx: usize\` — Index into the selected surfaces from stage 2.6.
466	- \`surface: OwnedPtr<HandleGeomSurface>\` — Infinite OCCT surface, type-erased (created in 3.1).
467	- \`adjacent_faces: Vec<usize>\` — Indices of adjacent FaceDescriptors, ordered topologically around this face's boundary. A face may appear multiple times if it shares multiple edges with this face (e.g., a cylindrical face adjacent to itself via a seam edge).
468	- \`edge_indices: Vec<usize>\` — ReconEdge indices. \`edge_indices[i]\` is the edge between this face and \`adjacent_faces[i]\`.
469	- \`vertex_indices: Vec<usize>\` — BRepVertex indices at corners. \`vertex_indices[i]\` is the vertex between \`edge_indices[i]\` and \`edge_indices[(i+1) % N]\`.
470	- The \`TopoDS_Face\` objects built from these descriptors live in \`Stage3Output.faces\` (parallel to \`face_descriptors\`), populated in 3.4, with \`Stage3Output.concave_faces\` flagging faces whose natural normal points into the material.
471	
472	A vector of \`ReconEdge\` structs:
473	- \`face_indices: [usize; 2]\` — Indices of the two adjacent FaceDescriptors.
474	- \`vertex_indices: [usize; 2]\` — Indices of the two BRepVertices at each end. For closed-loop edges (no vertices), both are \`usize::MAX\`.
475	- \`curve_3d: Option<OwnedPtr<HandleGeomCurve>>\` — 3D intersection curve, trimmed to vertex endpoints (populated in 3.3).
476	- \`pcurves: [Option<OwnedPtr<HandleGeom2dCurve>>; 2]\` — pcurve on each adjacent face's surface, parallel to \`face_indices\` (populated in 3.4 by the shared-edge assembler).
477	- \`tangent: bool\` — Whether the adjacent surfaces are tangent along this edge (detected in 3.2).
478	- \`mesh_boundary_vertices: Vec<usize>\` — Mesh vertex indices along this boundary, ordered along the boundary.
479	
480	A vector of \`BRepVertex\` structs:
481	- \`point: [f64; 3]\` — 3D position: the mesh vertex position, refined for sharp corners by cyclic projection onto the adjacent fitted surfaces (see stage 2.6 refitting notes).
482	- \`adjacent_faces: Vec<usize>\` — Indices of FaceDescriptors meeting at this vertex, in topological order.
483	- \`adjacent_edges: Vec<usize>\` — Indices of ReconEdges meeting at this vertex, in topological order.
484	- \`uv_coords: Vec<(f64, f64)>\` — UV coordinates of this vertex on each adjacent face's surface, parallel to \`adjacent_faces\` (populated in 3.4).
485	- Per-vertex tolerances live on the shared \`TopoDS_Vertex\` objects, raised adaptively in 3.4 to cover each vertex's distance from its curve endpoints.
486	
487	#### 3.1 Create OCCT surface objects and build adjacency graph
488	
489	**Surface creation** (\`create_occt_surface\`):
490	- For each selected surface, create the corresponding infinite OCCT surface:
491	    - Planar → \`geom::Plane::new_pnt_dir\` with \`gp::Pnt\` at centroid and \`gp::Dir\` along normal. Wrapped via \`to_handle().to_handle_surface()\`.
492	    - Cylindrical → \`geom::CylindricalSurface::new_ax3_real\` with \`gp::Ax3\` from axis origin/direction and radius. Wrapped via \`to_handle().to_handle_surface()\`.
493	    - Spherical → \`geom::SphericalSurface::new_ax3_real\` with \`gp::Ax3\` from center and Z-axis direction. Wrapped via \`to_handle().to_handle_surface()\`.
494	    - Conical → \`geom::ConicalSurface::new_ax3_real2\` with \`gp::Ax3\` from apex/direction and half-angle + radius at reference height. Wrapped via \`to_handle().to_handle_surface()\`. The \`Ax3\` origin is at the cone apex, Z along axis.
495	    - Toroidal → \`geom::ToroidalSurface::new_ax3_real2\` with \`gp::Ax3\` from center/axis direction, major radius $R$, and minor radius $r$. Wrapped via \`to_handle().to_handle_surface()\`. The \`Ax3\` origin is at the torus center, Z along rotation axis.
496	    - BSpline → a fresh handle to the exact \`Geom_BSplineSurface\` object fitted in stage 2.7 (never a reconstruction — the charts wrap the same object).
497	
498	- For consistency with OCCT conventions: planes use gp_Ax3 with Z as normal; cylinders have Z along axis; spheres have poles at Z extremes; cones have Z along axis from apex; tori have Z along rotation axis. This affects pcurve computation in 3.3.
499	
500	
501	**Adjacency graph construction:**
502	1. Build face-to-surface map: for each mesh face, record which selected surface it belongs to (\`build_face_to_surface_map\`).
503	2. Collect boundary edges: walk all mesh edges; when an edge separates faces belonging to different surfaces, record it as an \`UndirectedEdge\` keyed by \`SurfacePair\` (\`collect_boundary_edges\`).
504	3. Chain boundary edges: for each surface pair, chain connected boundary edges into ordered vertex sequences (\`chain_boundary_edges\`). Each chain becomes a \`ReconEdge\` with the mesh boundary vertices in order.
505	4. Find corner vertices: mesh vertices where 3+ selected surfaces meet become \`BRepVertex\` entries (\`find_corner_vertices\`). Their 3D position is taken from the mesh vertex.
506	5. Assign vertex indices to edges: for each \`ReconEdge\`, check if its first/last mesh boundary vertex is a corner vertex and set \`vertex_indices\` accordingly (or \`usize::MAX\` for closed-loop edges).
507	6. Build face descriptors: for each selected surface, create a \`FaceDescriptor\` with the OCCT surface handle. Walk the boundary edges, ordering them topologically around the face boundary (\`walk_edges_around_face\`). This uses a rotation-order algorithm: at each vertex, pick the next edge that rotates counter-clockwise around the face. This populates \`adjacent_faces\`, \`edge_indices\`, and \`vertex_indices\` for each face.
508	7. Build vertex adjacency: for each \`BRepVertex\`, walk the edges meeting at that vertex in topological order, recording \`adjacent_faces\` and \`adjacent_edges\`.
509	8. Validate topology: check Euler's formula V-E+F=2 for genus-0 models.
510	
511	- With the \`--compare\` flag (runs after stage 3.2 tangency detection):
512	    - For each \`ReconEdge\`, sample the mesh boundary vertices along the edge. For each sample vertex, compute the distance to the nearest edge/vertex in the reference STEP file using \`BRepExtrema_DistShapeShape\`. Report an error if any sample exceeds the edge's tolerance: \`--vertex-tolerance\` for sharp edges, \`--surface-tolerance\` for tangent edges (an imprecise tessellation can place the region boundary one row off a tangent junction — still within chordal tolerance of both surfaces and harmless, since tangent junction curves are built from the fitted hypotheses, not from these vertices).
513	    - For each \`BRepVertex\`, compute the distance from the vertex's 3D position to the nearest vertex in the reference STEP file. Report an error if any exceeds \`--vertex-tolerance\`.
514	    - These checks validate that the adjacency graph's topological features (edges where surfaces meet, corners where 3+ surfaces meet) correspond to real features in the reference model.
515	
516	#### 3.2 Detect tangency relationships
517	For each ReconEdge, determine whether the two adjacent surfaces are tangent along the shared boundary. This matters because \`GeomAPI_IntSS\` can fail or produce degenerate results for tangent/near-tangent surfaces, requiring special handling in edge curve computation (3.3).
518	
519	**Detection algorithm (implemented):**
520	- For each ReconEdge, sample mesh boundary vertices along the shared boundary (every 5th vertex, minimum 3 samples, always including first and last).
521	- At each sample point, compute the outward-facing surface normal of both adjacent surfaces analytically:
522	    - Planar: the hypothesis normal (constant).
523	    - Cylindrical: normalized radial direction from axis to point, negated if concave.
524	    - Spherical: normalized direction from center to point, negated if concave.
525	    - Conical: radial direction from axis at the point's axial position, rotated by the cone's half-angle away from the axis, negated if concave. Specifically: for point $p$ with axial component $h = (p-A)\cdot\hat{a}$, the normal is the unit vector in the plane of (radial, axis) at angle $(90° - \theta)$ from radial.
526	    - Toroidal: direction from the nearest tube center point to the surface point, negated if concave. The tube center is $c = C + R \cdot \hat{r}$ where $\hat{r}$ is the radial unit vector from the torus axis to the point.
527	
528	- Compute the dot product of the two normals. If all sampled pairs agree within 2° (dot > cos(2°) ≈ 0.9994), mark the edge as tangent.
529	- Points too close to a cylinder axis or sphere center (degenerate) cause the edge to be marked non-tangent.
530	
531	**Decision: do not modify surfaces to enforce tangency.** Modifying analytic surfaces would change the geometry. Instead, tangent edges get special handling in edge curve computation (3.3): construct the edge curve directly rather than relying on surface-surface intersection.
532	
533	Tangent edges arise from fillets and blends: part_rounded_cube_10_r2 has 8 plane-cylinder tangent edges, and rounded_cube_10_r2 has 46 tangent edges (plane-cylinder + sphere-cylinder). Toroidal fillets produce tangent edges at both boundaries (torus-plane, torus-cylinder, torus-torus).
534	
535	
536	- With the \`--compare\` flag:
537	    - For each ReconEdge marked as tangent, verify that the corresponding edge in the reference STEP file is also tangent (i.e., the two adjacent STEP surfaces have matching normals along the STEP edge). Report a warning if tangency is detected in the mesh but not in the STEP reference, or vice versa.
538	    - This is a consistency check, not a geometry check — stage 3.1's --compare already validates that mesh edge boundary vertices lie on STEP edges. Stage 3.2 adds validation that the tangency classification itself is correct.
539	
540	#### 3.3 Create edge curves
541	For each ReconEdge, compute the 3D intersection curve between the two adjacent surfaces, trim it to the vertex endpoints, and store in \`edge.curve_3d\`.
542	
543	**Intersection computation (implemented):**
544	- For non-tangent edges: use \`GeomAPI_IntSS\` to compute intersection curves with tolerance \`vertex_tolerance_mm\`. IntSS may return multiple curves (e.g., a plane cutting through a torus); \`select_closest_curve()\` picks the one closest to the mesh boundary vertices by sampling up to 10 evenly-spaced boundary vertices, projecting each onto each candidate curve via \`GeomAPI_ProjectPointOnCurve\`, and summing distances.
545	- For tangent edges: construct the curve analytically rather than using \`GeomAPI_IntSS\` (which fails or produces degenerate results for tangent/near-tangent surfaces). Dispatches by surface pair type:
546	  - **Plane-cylinder**: \`Geom_Line\` parallel to the cylinder axis at the tangent point, computed from the cylinder axis, radius, and the plane normal component perpendicular to the axis.
547	  - **Cylinder-cylinder**: \`Geom_Line\` along the first cylinder's axis direction.
548	  - **Sphere-cylinder**: \`Geom_Circle\` (great circle arc on the sphere). Center and radius come from the sphere hypothesis. The circle plane normal is \`normalize(cross(v0 - center, v1 - center))\`. Arc selection samples mesh boundary vertices to determine forward vs. reverse arc on the periodic circle. Open arcs use this path for slight cones too (the circle is built from sphere geometry + endpoint vertices, so it is valid for near-cylindrical cones such as fillets fitted with a small half-angle).
549	  - **Sphere-cone (closed loop)**: the tangent circle is NOT a great circle (it sits at radius R·cos α, offset along the cone axis). Constructed as a \`Geom_Circle\` perpendicular to the cone axis, with axial position and radius averaged from the edge's mesh boundary vertices (\`compute_tangent_edge_curve_axis_circle_closed\`) — the same vertex-averaging approach as plane-torus.
550	  - **Plane-cone** (TODO): \`Geom_Line\` along the cone's generator at the tangent azimuth, from the tangent point toward (or away from) the apex.
551	  - **Cylinder-cone** (TODO): \`Geom_Circle\` at the axial position where the cone radius equals the cylinder radius (when coaxial), or \`IntSS\`-based (non-coaxial).
552	  - **Plane-torus**: \`Geom_Circle\` perpendicular to the torus axis. Circle center and radius computed from mesh boundary vertices projected onto the torus axis. Handles both closed loops and arcs.
553	  - **Cylinder-torus**: \`Geom_Circle\` computed by the vertex-averaging algorithm against *both* candidate axes — the torus axis (coaxial fillet junctions) and the cylinder axis (tube-continuation junctions, e.g. a straight pipe leaving a pipe-elbow torus, where the junction is a tube cross-section) — keeping whichever circle best fits the boundary vertices.
554	  - **Torus-torus**: \`Geom_Circle\` perpendicular to the torus axis. Same vertex-averaging algorithm.
555	  - **Sphere-torus** (TODO): \`Geom_Circle\` — analogous to sphere-cylinder but the junction is a circle on the sphere.
556	  - **Fallback**: Construct a line from the two vertex endpoints.
557	
558	  All tangent edges are trimmed to vertex endpoints using the same parameter projection as non-tangent edges. A \`validate_tangent_curve\` step checks that boundary vertices lie within \`surface_tolerance_mm\` of the constructed curve.
559	
560	**Trimming to vertex endpoints (implemented):**
561	- For each ReconVertex at an edge endpoint, project the vertex's 3D position onto the intersection curve using \`GeomAPI_ProjectPointOnCurve\` to get the curve parameter value.
562	- Trim the curve to the parameter range \`[t_start, t_end]\` using \`Geom_TrimmedCurve\`.
563	- For closed-loop edges (vertex_indices == usize::MAX), use the full intersection curve parameter range.
564	- **Partial arc reconstruction**: When \`IntSS\` returns a partial arc for a closed-loop edge (e.g., a semicircle when a sphere-plane intersection passes through the sphere's UV poles), reconstruct the full circle from 3 sampled points on the arc using the circumscribed circle formula. This fixes cases where the intersection plane contains the sphere axis, causing \`IntSS\` to split the circle into two separate arcs in UV space.
565	- For non-periodic curves, ensure \`t_start < t_end\` (swap if needed).
566	- The resulting \`TrimmedCurve\` is upcast to \`HandleGeomCurve\` via \`.to_handle().to_handle_curve()\` and stored in \`edge.curve_3d\`.
567	
568	**Boundary-chain validation and repair (\`repair_edge_curve_from_boundary\`):**
569	- The mesh boundary chain lies on the true junction within chordal tolerance, so it is the ground truth for every edge curve regardless of surface pair. After trimming, the curve is validated against a spread of chain vertices; when it strays beyond \`--surface-tolerance\` — the complementary-arc trap on a periodic junction, a degenerate IntSS result with an odd parameterization, or a straight-line stand-in for a curved junction — the curve is rebuilt as the circle through three spread chain vertices, re-trimmed to the endpoint vertices with a chain-supported arc (\`select_arc_parameters\`), and adopted if it fits the chain better. This is what recovers \`cone_tangent_planes\`' two apex-cap boundary arcs (IntSS between its near-tangent coaxial cones returns degenerate curves; the repair reduces a 14 mm chain deviation to ~3e-6 mm).
570	- The chain is also the **last-resort constructor** (\`edge_curve_from_boundary_chain\`): when both the IntSS and tangent-analytic constructions fail outright, the edge curve is built from the chain directly (vertex-to-vertex line, upgraded to the chain's circle when curved) and accepted if it fits the chain within \`--surface-tolerance\`. Only edges where even the chain fails are left to fallback geometry, and those remain a stage-3.3 warning. (Recovers the two intersecting-fillet-cylinder junctions on \`stepped_block_toroidal_fillets_fine\` whose IntSS curves reject vertex projection.)
571	
572	**Pcurve computation (in stage 3.4):**
573	- Pcurves are computed analytically per (edge, face) incidence by the shared-edge assembler and attached to the shared \`TopoDS_Edge\` objects — see stage 3.4.
574	
575	**Seam edges (implemented in stage 3.4):**
576	- A face whose boundary wraps a periodic direction gets a seam edge along an isoline, with two pcurves (one per occurrence) on the shared edge — see stage 3.4. Complete closed surfaces with no boundary at all (full sphere, complete torus) use \`MakeFace\` natural bounds, which generates both seams of a complete torus automatically.
577	
578	**Vertex position consistency:**
579	- OCCT requires all edges at a vertex to share the same \`TopoDS_Vertex\`. All edges are built from the shared per-BRepVertex \`TopoDS_Vertex\` objects; vertex tolerances are raised adaptively to cover each vertex's distance from its curve endpoints (stage 3.4), and pcurve endpoints snap to the shared vertex's UV so wires close in 2D at their vertices.
580	
581	- With the \`--compare\` flag:
582	    - For each ReconEdge with a computed \`curve_3d\`, sample points along the trimmed curve (e.g., 10 evenly-spaced parameter values between start and end). For each sample point, compute the distance to the nearest edge in the reference STEP file using \`BRepExtrema_DistShapeShape\`. Report an error if any sample exceeds \`--vertex-tolerance\`.
583	    - Additionally, verify that the curve endpoints coincide with BRepVertex positions within \`--vertex-tolerance\`.
584	    - This validates that the computed intersection curves not only connect the right vertices but also follow the correct geometric path between them.
585	
586	#### 3.4 Create OCCT faces
587	For each ReconFace, construct a \`TopoDS_Face\` from the surface and the shared edges — **shared-edge topological assembly** (the §3.x rework, implemented). Every ReconEdge is exactly one \`TopoDS_Edge\` used by both adjacent faces; each face attaches its own pcurve to the shared edge and assembles its wires and the face directly with \`BRep_Builder\`. \`MakeWire\`/\`MakeFace\`-from-wire/\`ShapeFix\` (which copy edges and would break the sharing) are not used; one uniform mechanism covers planes, cylinders, cones, spheres, and tori alike, and is the intended foundation for NURBS faces.
588	
589	**Algorithm (as implemented):**
590	
591	*Step 1: Shared vertices and edges.*
592	- One \`TopoDS_Vertex\` per \`BRepVertex\` (tolerance \`vertex_tolerance_mm\`).
593	- One \`TopoDS_Edge\` per \`ReconEdge\` via \`BRepBuilderAPI_MakeEdge\` from the trimmed 3D curve with explicit parameters. For periodic curves (circles), vertex order is matched to the curve parameterization direction: V1 corresponds to \`first_parameter()\` and V2 to \`last_parameter()\`. This is critical because \`MakeEdge\` strips \`TrimmedCurve\` wrappers and uses \`ElCLib::AdjustPeriodic\` on the base periodic curve, which always selects the forward (CCW) arc from V1 to V2.
594	- **Adaptive vertex tolerance**: before each \`MakeEdge\`, each vertex's distance to its curve endpoint is computed and the OCCT vertex tolerance raised to cover it (1% margin) — intersection curves of imprecisely-fitted surfaces don't pass exactly through mesh vertex positions.
595	
596	*Step 2: Per-face UV analysis (\`scan_face\`).*
597	- Each face gets an analytic **chart** (\`SurfaceChart\`) mirroring the gp_Ax3 frame of its OCCT surface, with closed-form point→(U,V) and (U,V)→point maps for the five analytic surface types; B-spline faces carry a chart wrapping the exact fitted surface via a cached \`ShapeAnalysis_Surface\` (\`ValueOfUV\`/\`NextValueOfUV\` with a gap re-check that catches Newton settling in nearby local minima). All UV work — sampling, windows, orientation tests — is analytic; \`GeomAPI_ProjectPointOnSurf\` is no longer used in face construction.
598	- **Sphere gauge choice**: a sphere's axis is a free gauge, so it is pointed at the mesh region's mean centroid direction from the center. The region's boundary then never passes near a UV pole: poles land strictly inside the region (a polar cap — handled by a degenerate pole edge) or strictly outside. This one choice replaces all of the old pole-proximity reorientation and pole-vertex special cases.
599	- Each boundary edge's stored curve (read back from the \`TopoDS_Edge\`, so \`MakeEdge\` parameter adjustments are respected) is sampled to branch-continuous UV arrays; U values within \`pole_radius_eps\` of a chart pole are backfilled from the nearest well-defined sample.
600	- Boundary edges are grouped into loops by \`BRepVertex\` connectivity and each loop is **branch-aligned**: consecutive edges' UV endpoints are matched by whole-period shifts. The loop's net **winding** around each periodic direction classifies the face: a loop with winding ±1 wraps the surface. U-jumps across pole junctions (a boundary loop passing through a cone apex or sphere pole vertex) are recorded as pole gaps rather than winding.
601	- More than 2 winding loops on one face is a hard error: ≥3 winding loops cannot bound a *connected* region of a periodic surface (each winding loop separates it), so the condition means the face's hypothesis claimed disconnected mesh regions (observed on 3DBenchy — see TODO.md). The fix belongs upstream — split face hypotheses into mesh-connected components before face construction — not in the seam planner.
602	
603	*Step 3: Seam planning (\`plan_seam_anchors\`).*
604	- A face whose boundary winds a periodic direction needs a **seam edge**; its endpoints must be vertices of the winding loops. Anchors: an existing \`BRepVertex\` on the loop when there is one; otherwise the (single) closed-loop edge is rebuilt to start at the seam azimuth and gains an anchor vertex at both ends. Because the anchor vertex becomes part of the *shared* edge, every face using that edge sees the same anchor — cross-face consistency is forced by sharing, not by tolerances. Faces are planned in waves so chains of wrapped faces (e.g. plane–cylinder–torus stacks) adopt one consistent seam azimuth; a face whose winding loops carry incompatible pre-determined anchors is a hard error (edge splitting is the recorded future extension).
605	
606	*Step 4: Per-face assembly (\`assemble_face\`).*
607	- **Window**: wrapped faces span \`[c, c+2π]\` in the wrap direction, bordered by the seam at the anchor coordinate; other periodic directions use the circular-gap window over boundary samples with mesh-centroid majority disambiguation (\`circular_window\`). Winding loops are rotated to start at their anchor and oriented for a counterclockwise composite; plain loops are lifted into the window.
608	- **Seam edge**: 3D curve = \`Geom_Surface::u_iso\`/\`v_iso\` at the seam coordinate (parameterized exactly by the other coordinate, for every surface type), trimmed between the two anchors (or anchor and pole), built with \`MakeEdge\` + explicit parameters. It carries **two pcurves** (\`BRep_Builder::update_edge\` two-pcurve variant): PC1 for its FORWARD occurrence at the window's high border, PC2 for REVERSED at the low border (OCCT resolves FORWARD→PCurve1; verified against a \`BRepPrimAPI_MakeCylinder\` seam).
609	- **Degenerate pole edges**: at a cone apex or sphere pole the UV boundary closes across the pole with a degenerate edge — no 3D curve, \`degenerated\` flag, a constant-V pcurve line spanning the U gap, the pole vertex at both ends. Used both for wrapped pole caps (apex/pole interior to the face, e.g. \`cone_tangent_planes\`' apex-cap disks and \`simple_cone\`'s lateral face) and for pole gaps inside ordinary loops (a boundary through an apex vertex). This is the *only* pole mechanism, identical for cones and spheres.
610	- **Pcurves** (\`build_pcurve\`): for every (edge, face) incidence, UV samples of the edge's stored curve are interpolated (\`Geom2dAPI_Interpolate\`) with the curve's own parameters, so SameParameter holds by construction. Endpoint samples snap to the shared vertex's analytic UV (branch-lifted), making wires closed in 2D at their vertices even when a mesh vertex sits slightly off this face's fitted surface. Seam pcurves pin the wrap coordinate to the window borders exactly. Sampling densifies (33→257 points) until the pcurve reproduces the 3D curve within \`vertex_tolerance/10\` or the deviation plateaus (a 3D curve off the fitted surface by its fit residual cannot be represented away); the achieved deviation — measured at samples *and* midpoints, *including endpoints* — sets the edge tolerance (OCCT's in-context BRepCheck validates pcurve/curve agreement over the whole range against it). The pcurves are recorded in \`ReconEdge.pcurves\` and vertex UVs in \`BRepVertex.uv_coords\`.
611	- **Wires and orientation**: the composite wire of a wrapped face is emitted in counterclockwise window order (e.g. bottom loop +U, seam up, top loop −U, seam down). Plain loops are oriented by the mesh ground truth: the loop that winds around interior mesh-centroid UV points is the outer wire (counterclockwise); loops containing no interior points are holes (clockwise). Wires are \`BRep_Builder\`-built from the shared edges with per-occurrence orientations; the face is \`BRep_Builder::make_face\` + wires + \`natural_restriction(false)\`.
612	- **Faces with no boundary edges** (full sphere, complete torus) use \`MakeFace\` natural bounds — OCCT generates their seam/degenerate edges and there are no shared edges to preserve.
613	- A face is flagged **concave** when its hypothesis is concave: its natural surface normal points into the material, so stage 3.5 adds it to the shell REVERSED.
614	
615	*Step 5: Validate and compare.*
616	- Validate each face with \`BRepCheck_Analyzer\`; invalid faces get detailed \`BRepCheck_Face\` diagnostics plus a structural dump (wire order, orientations, pcurve endpoints, vertex parameters) under \`-v\`. Note \`BRepCheck_Analyzer::IsValid(face)\` reflects subshape statuses *in the face's context* (e.g. \`InvalidCurveOnSurface\`), which is why the edge tolerance accounting above matters.
617	- With \`--compare\`: for each face, sample a representative mesh face centroid from the surface's mesh faces and compute distance to the reference STEP shape using \`BRepExtrema_DistShapeShape\`. Report face count comparison. Error if max distance exceeds \`--surface-tolerance\`.
618	
619	#### 3.5 Construct shells
620	Assemble faces into shells directly from the shared-edge topology. Faces already share their \`TopoDS_Edge\` objects, so shells are **closed by construction** — \`BRepBuilderAPI_Sewing\` is no longer used, and \`ShapeAnalysis_Shell\` remains as validation only.
621	
622	**Algorithm (as implemented):**
623	- Group faces into connected components over the ReconEdge adjacency (multi-solid models produce one shell per component, e.g. hollow_cube's outer and void shells).
624	- For each component, \`BRep_Builder::make_shell\` and add every face — FORWARD for convex/planar faces, REVERSED for concave ones — then set the shell's closed flag. Orientation is consistent outward by construction: mesh normals point outward (stage 1 validation) and the hypothesis convexity flags encode each surface's relation to the material.
625	- Validate with \`ShapeAnalysis_Shell::CheckOrientedShells\` (free edges, orientation inconsistencies) and report.
626	
627	- With the \`--compare\` flag:
628	    - Count shells in the reference STEP file by exploring for \`Shell\` sub-shapes.
629	    - Report an error if any shell has free edges (an edge used by only one face), which would indicate broken shared-edge topology.
630	    - Report shell count comparison and orientation check results.
631	
632	#### 3.6 Construct solids
633	Convert closed shells into \`TopoDS_Solid\` objects.
634	
635	**Algorithm:**
636	- For each shell, use \`ShapeFix_Solid::SolidFromShell\` which handles orientation automatically.
637	- Apply \`ShapeFix_Shape::Perform()\` for comprehensive face/wire/edge/shell/solid fixes.
638	- Call \`BRepLib::SameParameter\` with \`forced=false\` and tight tolerance (\`vertex_tolerance_mm\`) to ensure pcurve/3D curve agreement without corrupting already-correct pcurves. Using \`forced=true\` or a large tolerance (e.g. 1.0mm) recomputes all pcurves and can corrupt faces with closely-spaced tangent edges (e.g. sphere faces at sphere-cylinder junctions), causing BRepCheck self-intersection failures.
639	- Call \`BRepLib::UpdateTolerances\` and \`BRepLib::OrientClosedSolid\`.
640	- Validate with \`BRepCheck_Analyzer\`. Log warning if validation fails (may indicate upstream face issues from stage 3.4).
641	- Compute and report volume using \`BRepGProp::VolumeProperties\`.
642	- **Multi-shell solids (internal voids)**: after per-shell solids are built, \`merge_void_solids\` finds solids whose bounding box is contained in a strictly larger solid's box; each such void's shells are added to the container solid (\`BRep_Builder::Add\`), trying both shell orientations and keeping the one whose resulting volume equals container − Σ voids. The hollow_cube test model (tests/occt/, generated by \`gen-test-models\` since ccad cannot express enclosed voids) validates one solid of 7000 mm³ instead of two nested solids.
643	
644	- With the \`--compare\` flag:
645	    - Count solids in the reference STEP file.
646	    - Compute volumes of both constructed and reference solids. Match by sorted volume. Report a hard **error** if relative volume difference exceeds 1% (a warning until 2026-07-08; a warning-only check let a 46% volume regression slip past a green test suite). Most models achieve volume agreement within ~1e-7 relative difference.
647	    - Compute \`BRepExtrema_DistShapeShape\` between each constructed solid and the reference STEP shape. Report error if distance exceeds \`--surface-tolerance\`. This is the tightest geometric accuracy check.
648	
649	#### 3.x Rework: shared-edge topological assembly instead of geometric sewing
650	
651	**Status: IMPLEMENTED (2026-07-08).** Stages 3.4/3.5 above describe the result; this section records the original motivation and what the rework changed, since it reshaped stage 3's architecture.
652	
653	**The old design and why it was fragile.** Stage 3.4 used to build each face independently (\`MakeWire\`/\`MakeFace\`/UV-bounds/\`ShapeFix\` paths) and stage 3.5 reconciled them with \`BRepBuilderAPI_Sewing\`, a *geometric* stitch that merges edges by proximity within a tolerance. Edges were created once as shared \`TopoDS_Edge\` objects, but \`MakeFace\`/\`MakeWire\`/\`ShapeFix\` copy edges, and the UV-bounds paths synthesized their own boundary edges — so the sharing was defeated by assembly time and sewing was load-bearing. Every hard case — sphere pole caps, torus seams, cone apex caps, tangent-edge tolerance relaxations — was a symptom of trimming faces independently and matching them by geometric tolerance. \`cone_tangent_planes\` was the sharpest example: its two apex-cap cone faces (full disks whose boundary circles are split into two arcs by two vertices, with the apex an interior U-pole) could not be expressed by any of the per-case paths, and a pure UV-bounds cap broke sewing (free edges → open shell).
654	
655	**What the rework did (all in stage 3.4/3.5 above):**
656	1. Each edge is built once (shared \`TopoDS_Edge\` + shared vertices, with its 3D curve) and *stays* the same object in both faces.
657	2. For both adjacent faces of every edge, the pcurve on that face's surface is computed analytically and attached to the shared edge (\`ReconEdge.pcurves\` is now populated, as is \`BRepVertex.uv_coords\`).
658	3. Degenerate edges at UV poles (cone apex, sphere pole) and seam edges at periodic wraps are first-class topology built by one uniform mechanism (winding classification → seam anchors on shared vertices → iso-curve seam with two pcurves; pole gaps → degenerate edges).
659	4. Faces and shells are assembled directly with \`BRep_Builder\`; shells are closed by construction. \`BRepBuilderAPI_Sewing\` and all \`ShapeFix\` calls in face construction are gone (stage 3.6's solid-level fixing remains).
660	5. Stage 3.3 gained boundary-chain validation/repair of edge curves — the mesh boundary chain is ground truth for every junction, which is also what fixed \`cone_tangent_planes\`' degenerate cone-cone IntSS curves.
661	
662	Result: full test suite green, \`cone_tangent_planes\` volume matches to ~1e-7 relative (was 60% off), \`stepped_block_toroidal_fillets\` volume improved (2.7e-3 → 1.6e-3 relative), and every remaining pole/seam behavior is a consequence of the uniform mechanism rather than a per-surface special case. The NURBS effort (§2.7) can now add freeform faces by supplying a chart (point→UV map) and reusing the whole assembler unchanged.
663	
664	**Hard OCCT constraints (established empirically — do not relearn by crashing).**
665	- \`ShapeFix_*\` can **segfault on cone faces** (pcurve/edge-curve interaction). The assembler attaches pcurves directly with \`BRep_Builder\` and never routes faces through ShapeFix; do not reintroduce ShapeFix on cone faces to "clean up" a wire. (Stage 3.6's \`ShapeFix_Solid\`/\`ShapeFix_Shape\` on assembled solids has been safe.)
666	- \`MakeEdge\` on a periodic (circle) curve forces p1 < p2 and takes the forward arc V1→V2, so **vertex order selects which arc** — see the \`d0_start\`/\`d1_start\` block in \`create_occt_faces_all\`. The same trap applies when *trimming* periodic curves in stage 3.3; the boundary-chain repair is the safety net.
667	- \`BRep_Tool::CurveOnSurface\` resolves a seam edge's FORWARD occurrence to PCurve1 and REVERSED to PCurve2; OCCT's own cylinder puts the FORWARD occurrence at the high U border (verified via \`BRepPrimAPI_MakeCylinder\`).
668	- \`Geom_Surface::u_iso(u)\`/\`v_iso(v)\` return curves parameterized exactly by the other surface coordinate (verified in ElSLib for cone/sphere/torus/cylinder) — this is what makes seam construction surface-type-agnostic.
669	- Thread-safety of the OCCT calls used here: LEARNINGS "OCCT Thread Safety" (BRepExtrema, MakeEdge/Face/Vertex, ShapeFix_* are all safe; only STEP Reader/Writer need serializing).
670	
671	---
672	
673	### Stage 4: Output
674	
675	#### 4.1 Output objects
676	Write constructed solid(s) to a STEP file.
677	
678	**Algorithm (implemented):**
679	- Build a \`topo_ds::Compound\` containing all solids from stage 3.6.
680	- Set STEP metadata: AP214 schema, product name "brepper" via \`Interface_Static::set_c_val\`.
681	- Create a \`STEPControl_Writer\`, transfer the compound with \`StepModelType::Asis\` mode to preserve exact geometry.
682	- Write the output file. Report error if transfer or write returns non-Done status.
683	- With the \`--compare\` flag (each check is a hard error as of 2026-07-08 — warning-only volume checks let a large regression slip past a green suite):
684	    - Re-read the written STEP file via \`STEPControl_Reader\` to validate the round-trip. Error if unreadable (note this makes \`-o /dev/null\` incompatible with \`--compare\`; \`run_test_table.sh\` writes a real temp file).
685	    - Compare volumes of the output shape vs reference shape using \`BRepGProp::VolumeProperties\`. Error on >1% relative difference.
686	    - Compute maximum distance between output and reference using \`BRepExtrema_DistShapeShape\`. Error if exceeds \`--surface-tolerance\`.
687	
688	---
689	
690	## Visualization (\`--viz-stages\`)
691	
692	The \`--viz-stages\` flag enables interactive 3D visualization for debugging each pipeline stage. The user specifies a stage pattern (e.g. \`2.2\` for conical BFS, or \`2.*\` for all stage 2 sub-stages). The companion \`--viz-stages-level\` flag (default 1) controls how often the visualization pauses: level 1 pauses at committed hypotheses and stage 2.6 selections, level 2 also pauses at each accepted seed, and level 3 also pauses at each individual BFS face acceptance.
693	
694	### Architecture
695	
696	The visualization uses a two-thread architecture with synchronous channel communication:
697	
698	1. **Main thread**: Owns the window, drives the three-d render loop, and displays overlays.
699	2. **Pipeline thread**: Runs the brepper pipeline (stages 1–4) in the background.
700	
701	### Communication pipeline
702	
703	\`\`\`
704	Pipeline thread                          Main/render thread
705	     │                                         │
706	     │  VizOverlay (via SyncSender)             │
707	     ├────────────────────────────────────────►  │
708	     │                                         │ display overlay, wait for keypress
709	     │  VizAction (via SyncSender)              │
710	     │  ◄────────────────────────────────────────┤
711	     │                                         │
712	     ▼                                         ▼
713	\`\`\`
714	
715	- \`VizSender::show_and_wait(overlay)\` sends a \`VizOverlay\` to the render thread and blocks.
716	- The render thread displays face highlights, translucent geometry overlays (cylinders, cones, spheres, tori), status text, and optionally moves the camera.
717	- The user presses **Space** (NextStep), **Shift+Space** (NextSeed/skip to next seed), or **Q** (Quit).
718	- The render thread sends back a \`VizAction\` and the pipeline thread resumes.
719	
720	### Key types (in \`src/viz.rs\`)
721	
722	- \`VizSender\` / \`VizReceiver\`: Channel endpoints. Created by \`viz_channel()\`.
723	- \`VizOverlay\`: A display frame containing face highlights, cylinder/cone/sphere/torus overlays, line overlays, shape meshes, status text, and optional camera focus.
724	- \`VizAction\`: The user's response — \`NextStep\`, \`NextSeed\`, \`SkipToSeed(face_index)\`, or \`Quit\`.
725	- \`FaceHighlight\`, \`CylinderOverlay\`, \`ConeOverlay\`, \`SphereOverlay\`, \`TorusOverlay\`, \`LineOverlay\`, \`ShapeMeshOverlay\`: Overlay primitives.
726	
727	### Interactive features
728	
729	The render window (powered by three-d with egui overlay) provides:
730	- **Camera controls**: Left-drag orbit, right-drag pan, scroll zoom (toward cursor).
731	- **Face numbers** (\`N\` key): Thin black labels at face centroids, culled by face-normal direction (back-facing labels are hidden). The hovered face number is displayed larger with a white halo.
732	- **Hover info**: Ray-cast face picking under the cursor displays face index, vertex count, area, and normal in the bottom-left corner. Thick bright-yellow edges are drawn around the hovered face.
733	- **Click-to-skip**: Left-clicking a face sends \`SkipToSeed(face_index)\`, causing the BFS loop to skip ahead to the seed containing that face. Click is only processed while the window is paused waiting for user input (\`waiting_for_input = true\`) to avoid a deadlock where the BFS thread would block trying to send the next overlay while the render loop also blocks waiting for input.
734	- **Help overlay** (\`?\` key): Shows all keyboard and mouse shortcuts.
735	
736	### Integration with generic BFS
737	
738	The \`SurfaceType\` trait provides:
739	- \`viz_overlays(fit, mesh, face_list)\`: Returns geometry overlays (e.g., a translucent cylinder for conical fits, a sphere for spherical fits).
740	- \`viz_fit_description(fit)\`: Returns a short text description of the fit parameters for the status bar.
741	
742	The generic \`generate_hypotheses()\` function in \`src/stage2/bfs.rs\` sends viz overlays controlled by \`--viz-stages-level\`:
743	- **Level 1** (default): Pause only after a hypothesis is committed (best trial selected and assigned to faces); also pause at stage 2.6 selection overlays. Least disruptive — good for checking overall results.
744	- **Level 2**: Also pause after each accepted seed (before BFS expansion begins). Useful to inspect initial fit quality.
745	- **Level 3**: Also pause after each individual BFS face acceptance during region growing. Very granular; use for tracing specific expansion decisions.
746	
747	BFS debug verbosity (controlled by \`--verbose\`) uses consistent indentation (no \`[prefix]\` tags):
748	- **Level 1**: Stage summaries (hypothesis counts, timing).
749	- **Level 2**: Per-hypothesis details (seed face list, final fit parameters).
750	- **Level 3**: Per-committed-hypothesis progress (seed/fit counts, profiling stats).
751	- **Level 4**: Per-seed-attempt details (seed rejected/accepted with error values, fit descriptions).
752	- **Level 5**: Per-BFS-face exploration details (each face accepted, rejected, or skipped with error values).
753	
754	---
755	
756	## Source Code Organization
757	
758	Each stage will be represented by a function which takes a ref to a configuration data structure and consumes an input data structure, and returns an output data structure (or error). That data structure is passed to the next stage:
759	
760	- main:
761	  - let config = parse flags, load --compare STEP file
762	  - out1 = stage1(config).unwrap()
763	  - if config.stage < 2: exit
764	  - out2 = stage2(congig, out1).unwrap()
765	  - if config.stage < 3: exit
766	  - out3 = stage3(config, out2).unwrap()
767	  - if config.stage < 4: exit
768	  - stage4(config, out3).unwrap()  # writes output file
769	- stageN:
770	  - execute whatever portions of stage1 are allowed by config.stage
771	
772	Each stage should have a source file stageN.rs, with a definition of that stage's output data structure at the top, the stageN() function, and then whatever other functions are required to implement the stage's functionality.
773	
774	---
775	
776	## Testing Strategy
777	
778	The main testing strategy is to process a set of example stl/step pairs, and use the --compare flag to ensure that the pipeline is working correctly for each. 
779	
780	1. **Unit Tests**: Individual components (readers, fitters, converters)
781	2. **Integration Tests**: Full pipeline on known geometries
782	3. **Regression Tests**: Compare output STEP to reference
783	4. **Validation**: Round-trip test (STEP → mesh → STEP)
784	
785	### Test Cases
786	
787	See [tests/RESULTS.md](tests/RESULTS.md) for the full test results table.
788	
789	Generated by: \`./scripts/run_test_table.sh [path/to/brepper]\`
790	
791	
792	---
793	
794	## Performance Targets
795	
796	| Mesh Size | Target Time |
797	|-----------|-------------|
798	| < 10K triangles | < 5 seconds |
799	| 10K - 100K triangles | < 30 seconds |
800	| 100K - 1M triangles | < 5 minutes |
801	
802	---
803	
804	## Future Enhancements
805	
806	- [ ] Support for OBJ, PLY input formats
807	- [ ] IGES export option
808	- [ ] Hole detection and filling
809	- [ ] Feature recognition (holes, pockets, bosses)
810	- [ ] Curvature-based surface detection: use per-vertex principal curvatures (Rusinkiewicz estimator) as an alternative seeding signal. Principal curvature signatures: plane (k1≈k2≈0), sphere (k1≈k2, constant), cylinder (k1=const, k2≈0), cone (k1 varies, k2≈0), torus (k1=const, k2 varies).
811	- [ ] Quadric pre-classification: fit 10-coefficient quadric via SVD as a fast O(N) surface classifier before expensive nonlinear fitting. Eigenvalue pattern of the 3×3 quadric matrix directly classifies plane/sphere/cylinder/cone.
812	- [ ] Adaptive mesh density handling: current BFS tolerance assumes roughly uniform tessellation. For meshes with varying triangle density (e.g., adaptive refinement near features), weight BFS decisions by triangle area.
813
```

> 
> Then there's a cascade of validation rules: normal-axis consistency checks during cone BFS, aggregate variance thresholds, angular coverage validation with special handling for cylinders and fillets, solid-angle eigenvalue tests for spheres, minor-circle coverage from normals for tori, minimum face counts and vertex requirements for tori with hop-expansion seeding, greedy area-based selection to resolve overlaps, absorb passes with tight and loose rules, boundary refinement with fixpoint iteration, and various tolerance multipliers for refitting.
> 
> Cylinder and cone fits are unified and pick the lower max vertex error, with half-angle bounds between 2° and 85°. Special seeds include conical fans, spherical 1-rings, and toroidal 1-rings with hop-expansion.

> The main failure modes I'm seeing are fragmentation from incorrect early hypotheses, greedy selection creating disconnected regions, and order-dependent selection behavior. is generation with local repairs. The core issue is that decisions get locked in too early without enough context, then require downstream fixes. Within a single type, committing a face to a hypothesis immediately blocks it from joining other hypotheses of that type — so a small bogus surface can monopolize faces that should form the real large surface. Across types, the ordering matters because cross-type mismatches are cheaper to prevent upfront than to untangle later. The greedy area selection approach resolves overlaps but breaks connectivity and sometimes picks the wrong surface.
> 
> The classical literature frames this as the primitive-detection problem: RANSAC-based methods use probabilistic seeding but still commit greedily, while region-growing approaches with variational refinement alternate between partitioning faces and refitting surfaces, and global energy minimization methods try to avoid local commitment altogether. In an exact mesh setting where vertices lie precisely on CAD surfaces and seam edges mark face boundaries, the true segmentation becomes a partition of the dual graph with boundaries aligned to mesh edges — every region is connected and well-defined. Since the curvature signal is noise-free, junction detection works reliably, and any seed placed on a surface should grow to encompass its entire region within vertex tolerance. The failure modes then stem from two sources: accidental fits where a subset of faces on one surface happens to lie exactly on a different surface type (like bands on spheres that also fit cones or tori), and greedy algorithms committing to the wrong choice locally before seeing the full picture. To fix this, I could decouple hypothesis generation from assignment — generate multiple candidate surfaces per face without immediately claiming them, then solve a global optimization problem where each face selects the best label based on fit error and boundary smoothness, using techniques like α-expansion with iterative refinement to guarantee connectivity.
> 
> Alternatively, I could flip the order entirely: partition the mesh first into smooth regions using feature detection (dihedral angles and curvature jumps to find seams), then classify each region by trying surface types in order of complexity (plane, cylinder, cone, sphere, torus, NURBS) to find the best fit.
> 
> This avoids the hypothesis proliferation problem because regions are locked in by the segmentation step—no cross-type competition. If a region doesn't fit any standard surface, I can recursively split it along the strongest curvature discontinuity, or merge adjacent regions if the segmentation was too fine. The main risk is that segmentation errors become permanent, but the exact-mesh assumption means edges should be detectable, and the split/merge validation acts as a safety valve.
> 
> For tangent junctions specifically, the curvature jump is the key signal—the junction edges themselves exist in the mesh, so the real question is whether I can reliably identify which edges mark the junction. > 
> This eliminates the ordering dependency, type-specific seeding masks, cross-type exclusion rules, and most of the acceptance heuristics that exist mainly to suppress spurious cross-type fits. > The core issue is that the system makes assignment decisions at three separate points without coordinating them globally. First, during generation within each type, the earliest hypothesis to claim a face locks it in, which causes small fits to fragment larger surfaces prematurely. Second, the cross-type stage ordering (sphere/torus before cone) creates conflicts because band patterns on spheres align exactly with cone and torus geometry. Third, the greedy area selection ignores connectivity and relies on post-hoc repairs like absorb and boundary refinement, which introduced measurable error. These three mechanisms operate independently, each with local patches that don't address the fundamental coordination problem.
> 
> The real opportunity is recognizing that the mesh itself encodes the true segmentation—the tessellator's edges mark exact boundaries, including at tangencies. Rather than treating this as a noisy surface-fitting problem, I should recover a partition of the face graph where each connected region fits exactly one surface type within tolerance. Accidental fits (where subsets of one surface lie exactly on another) drive both fragmentation and performance issues, and the current heuristics are just ad-hoc defenses against them. The principled solution is model selection at the region level: fit all types to the maximal region and pick the simplest that validates, since accidental fits are inherently sub-optimal.
> 
> Option A flips the approach entirely—segment first by classifying edges for sharp dihedrals or G2 curvature jumps, then fit each maximal region with ascending model complexity (plane through NURBS), accepting the simplest valid fit. Regions that fail all fits get recursively split at their strongest curvature discontinuity, and adjacent regions with compatible fits merge to repair over-segmentation. This eliminates the per-type BFS, seed selection, cross-type masks, and most heuristics, leaving connectivity guaranteed by construction.
> 
> Option B keeps the hypothesis generation via BFS but makes it non-committal—faces stay unowned while hypotheses accumulate support—then solves a global labeling problem by iteratively assigning each face to its best hypothesis (with boundary-length and smoothness penalties), refitting, and dropping empty hypotheses.  This turns the greedy approach into a variational loop that's the real mechanism, though it risks hypothesis proliferation and needs deduplication and suppression strategies to avoid combinatorial explosion. Option C stays with BFS but grows type-agnostically using a unified fitter framework instead of changing the partitioner itself. I'm going to reorganize around these axes: Option 1 is segmentation-first with per-region model selection—the most substantial restructuring but removes a lot of heuristic logic. Option 2 is the non-committal approach. Comparing Options 1 and 3, I realize Option 3's smooth-region BFS still needs edge classification to handle tangent junctions properly—a BFS growing on smoothness alone will cross fillet-to-plane boundaries without the G2 signal to stop it. So both approaches collapse into the same requirement: partition via edge classification (sharp edges as dihedral, smooth junctions as curvature jumps), then extract regions as connected components. This is really just criterion-driven BFS rather than fit-driven growth, which simplifies the whole thing—I'll call it "fit-free partitioning" and explore why that beats the fit-driven approach.

> The key advantage is avoiding fragmentation: fits can't fragment regions they never partition in the first place, classification of a maximal region inherently rejects accidental fits (a cone can't explain a whole sphere), and each region gets fit only a few times instead of once per seed, which kills the performance problem without needing the large_sphere mask. ## 🤖 Assistant
<!-- msg_011Cd8C3asFm8siXrjkjbVyc -->

I've read all four docs, the full 234-commit history, and the relevant stage-2 code. Here's my assessment and a set of options for the plan.

## Diagnosis: why the heuristics keep accumulating

The commit history tells a consistent story: nearly every hard-won stage-2 fix (stage reordering, junction masks, the large_sphere mask, angular/solid-angle/minor-circle coverage tests, normal-axis variance checks, the absorb pass, boundary refinement) is a countermeasure against one of two root causes, neither of which is attacked directly:

**1. Assignment is decided greedily, three separate times, with local information.** A face's fate is decided (a) during BFS — first hypothesis of a type to reach a face owns it, so a bogus 6-face cylinder band committed early fragments the real sphere (nosecone), and a bogus small fit splits the real cone into fragments that absorb must rejoin later (nosecone_truncated's 31-face piece); (b) across types by stage ordering — spherical before conical exists solely because bands on spheres lie exactly on cones; (c) at greedy area selection — which then breaks the connectivity invariant BFS established (face 5841's three disconnected regions) because it keeps only a winner's *still-unassigned remnant*. Stages 2.8/2.9 are post-hoc repairs for mistakes made at (a)–(c), and 2.8's loose rule actively introduced 0.4 mm slop.

**2. Accidental exact fits are fought hypothesis-by-hypothesis instead of region-by-region.** Every quad-row band on a sphere lies exactly on some torus and cone. Fit-driven growth from per-face seeds *invites* these sub-region fits, then needs acceptance heuristics to kill them and masks to avoid the 130–360× slowdowns (pill_fine: 1.2 s → 429 s without large_sphere). But an accidental fit is by definition **sub-region**: a cone can never explain a whole sphere. Fit the *maximal region* once and model-select, and the entire class of countermeasures becomes unnecessary.

The under-exploited asset is your own verified assumption set: the mesh is exact, **and the 2026-07-17 nosecone probe proved tessellators emit seam edges along B-rep face boundaries even at tangencies**. So the true answer is a partition of the face graph whose boundaries are *mesh edges already present in the input*, every region is connected, and any interior seed fits its whole region within vertex tolerance. That's a segmentation-and-classification problem, not the fit-things-in-noise problem (RANSAC-lineage) the current design resembles. The junction-mask work already demonstrated the key signal (G2 curvature jump catches tangent junctions that dihedral angle can't) — but it's used only as a *seeding hint*, not as the partition itself.

## Options for the plan rewrite

### Option 1 — Segmentation-first, classify-per-region (full back-to-basics; recommended)

Replace §2.1–2.6 + 2.8 with:

- **2.A Edge classification**: label every mesh edge boundary/interior. Boundary = sharp dihedral (existing check) OR G2 curvature jump (the junction-mask math, moved from faces to edges, keeping the local-relative threshold). No fitting involved.
- **2.B Region extraction**: connected components over interior edges. Regions are maximal, connected by construction, and each is fit O(1) times instead of per-seed.
- **2.C Region classification**: per region, fit the ladder plane → cylinder → cone → sphere → torus → NURBS in ascending DOF; accept the *simplest* surface that validates every vertex within tolerance. This is where the existing fitters (which are good — LM, medial-axis torus, the SOR (h,r) machinery) get reused unchanged, and where SURFACE_FITTING.md's "Unified SOR Framework" finally becomes the architecture instead of an appendix.
- **2.D Repair loop** (validation-driven, replaces absorb + subsumes boundary refinement): a region failing all fits *splits* at its strongest internal curvature discontinuity; adjacent regions whose fitted surfaces agree in parameters *merge*; boundary faces relabel to the neighbor surface that fits ≥2× better (current 2.9 logic, now the mechanism for ±1-row edge-classification errors at coarse tangent junctions rather than a patch).

What this deletes from the plan: stage ordering and its rationale paragraph, all seeding masks (junction, large_sphere), curvature-guided seed ordering, per-type seed topologies, the coverage acceptance tests (angular, solid-angle, minor-circle, normal-axis variance — accidental fits die because they can't explain the full region), greedy selection, absorb, and the single-fused-quad orphan gap (a region is a region regardless of face count; one quad classifies as a plane on the first rung). pipe_elbow's torus — which needed strip-merge seeding — becomes free: the torus is one smooth region and the fitter sees all of it at once. Expect a large stage-2 speedup on big models (full stage 2 on 3DBenchy is ~15 min today; planar-only is ~3 s).

Risks: edge classification at coarse tessellation (4-row fillets) will occasionally miss/over-fire — that's what 2.D absorbs; and truly G2-continuous freeform-freeform junctions merge into one NURBS region, which is an acceptable reconstruction. Migration is low-risk because stage 3 consumes `SelectedSurface` and doesn't care how it was produced: build 2.A/2.B behind a flag, and note that `--compare` can gate the *partition itself* (two mesh faces in one region must project to the same reference STEP face) before any fitting lands.

### Option 2 — Keep BFS, make assignment global (PEARL-style multi-model fitting)

Keep per-type hypothesis generation but make it **non-committal** (hypotheses record support; no face ownership during growth). Replace 2.6/2.8/2.9 with one iterated labeling loop: assign each face to its best-fitting hypothesis with a boundary-length smoothness penalty → refit each surviving hypothesis from its support → split labels into connected components → drop empties → repeat to convergence. This is the "energy-based geometric multi-model fitting" (Isack & Boykov PEARL) shape: global competition means the big correct surface beats every band everywhere, and connectivity is enforced each round. Keeps the most code; the ordering/masks stay initially as performance aids and can be retired by measurement (the pattern you used for multiface_planar). Risk: hypothesis proliferation is still the cost center — you'd need parameter-space dedup of same-surface hypotheses (which would also kill split fragments at generation time) and an "already explained" suppression to avoid the sphere-tiling blowup. It fixes fragmentation and the face-5841 family, but the accidental-fit *generation* cost remains structural.

### Option 3 — Invariant hardening only (triage; prerequisite for either option above)

No restructuring: (1) split every selected surface into mesh-connected components before stage 3 (fixes the 3-winding-loop abort family); (2) fix the single-fused-quad planar-hypothesis gap, then delete the loose absorb rule (already planned in TODO); (3) minimum-feature-size gate relative to surface tolerance (the r=0.2 mm cylinder vs 0.4 mm tolerance problem); (4) parameter-space hypothesis dedup so split fragments merge at generation, shrinking absorb's tight rule toward deletion. Worth doing regardless — items 1–3 are the current 3DBenchy blockers — but it leaves the heuristic treadmill in place.

## Recommendation

Option 1, staged: land Option 3's items 1–3 first (they unblock 3DBenchy and are invariants any architecture needs), then build 2.A/2.B as a new flag-gated partitioner validated by `--compare` against reference STEP face identity, then move classification onto regions and start deleting heuristics as the corpus proves them redundant — the measured-retirement workflow you already used for the seeding masks. Option 2 is the fallback if edge classification proves unreliable on coarse meshes, but it treats the symptom (competition between hypotheses) rather than the cause (generating sub-region hypotheses at all), and its code-preservation advantage is smaller than it looks since the fitters — the genuinely reusable part — carry over to Option 1 unchanged.

```markdown
# STL to STEP Conversion Utility - Development Plan

## Project Overview

**brepper** (B-Rep from Mesh) - A utility to convert triangulated STL meshes from CAD exports into parametric STEP files with fitted analytic and freeform surfaces.

## Design Principles

The stage 2 architecture was rewritten (2026-07-17) from a fit-driven BFS
hypothesis pipeline to **partition-then-classify** segmentation. These
principles drive the design; the old pipeline and the reasons it was retired
are recorded in [Abandoned Approaches](#abandoned-approaches).

1. **Exactness is the superpower.** Mesh vertices lie on the true CAD
   surfaces within numerical epsilon. A surface fitted to *all* faces of a
   region either validates every vertex within `--vertex-tolerance` or the
   region is not that surface — validation is decisive, not statistical. We
   are not solving the fit-primitives-in-noisy-scans problem the literature
   (RANSAC lineage) addresses.
2. **The answer's boundaries are already in the mesh.** CAD tessellators emit
   seam edges along B-rep face boundaries — *including tangent junctions*
   (verified 2026-07-17 on the nosecone cone–sphere tangency: 0 of 1572 STL
   triangles straddle the tangency ring; 104 vertices lie exactly on it). So
   the true segmentation is a partition of the mesh face graph whose region
   boundaries are a subset of mesh edges, and every region is connected.
3. **Partition first, fit later.** Segmentation uses fit-free signals
   (dihedral angles, discrete-curvature jumps). Fitting happens once per
   *maximal* region. This makes accidental exact fits — a band of sphere
   faces lying exactly on some cone or torus — structurally irrelevant: a
   sub-region surface can never explain the full region, so it is never even
   hypothesized. The abandoned pipeline spent most of its heuristics (stage
   ordering, seeding masks, coverage acceptance tests) and much of its
   runtime fighting exactly these fits.
4. **Repair by validation, locally.** Segmentation mistakes surface as
   classification failures and are repaired by local operations — split a
   region that fits nothing, merge adjacent regions that fit one surface,
   relabel boundary faces to the better-fitting side. No global fix-up
   passes with chordal slop.
5. **Invariants are enforced, not assumed.** Before stage 3: every mesh face
   belongs to exactly one region; every region is mesh-connected; every
   region's surface validates its own faces; results are deterministic.
   (The abandoned pipeline's greedy selection violated connectivity —
   3DBenchy face 5841 reached stage 3.4 claiming three disconnected mesh
   regions.)

## Assumptions

- Assumes the vertices of the mesh lie precisely on the CAD surfaces, modulo numerical error/stability.
- Assumes transitions between shapes always have a seam — verified to hold
  even for tangent transitions (see Design Principles #2).
- Assumes manifold mesh (each edge shared by exactly 2 faces).
- Assumes consistent face orientation (all normals point outward).

Fails noisily if any of the assumptions are violated, after stage 1.2's
repair pass has fixed what can be fixed without moving a vertex
(`--strict-mesh` disables repair).

## Dependencies

| Library | Purpose | Version |
|---------|---------|---------|
| OpenCASCADE (OCCT) | STL import, B-Rep modeling, surface fitting, STEP export | latest |

Use the opencascade binding in ../opencascade-rs/crates/opencascade-sys, including porting instructions in PORTING.md and details on the FFI mapping in ../opencascade-rs/crates/opencascade-binding-generator/README.md. It's a reasonably complete binding of the C++ API of the opencascade library which resides in ../opencascade-rs/crates/occt-sys/OCCT, with source code in src/ and documentation in dox/.

### Required OCCT Functionality

This section enumerates the OCCT classes and functions needed, for evaluating Rust binding coverage.

**Stage 1: Mesh Input**
- `RWStl` / `RWStl_Reader` — Read binary/ASCII STL files
- `gp_Pnt` — 3D point representation
- `gp_Dir` / `gp_Vec` — Direction and vector types

**Stage 2: Segmentation & Classification**
- `gp_Pln` — Plane geometry (normal + distance representation)
- `gp_Cylinder` / `gp_Ax3` — Cylindrical surface geometry (axis + radius)
- `gp_Sphere` — Spherical surface geometry (center + radius)
- `gp_Cone` — Conical surface geometry (axis + half-angle)
- `gp_Torus` — Toroidal surface geometry (for fillets)
- `GeomAPI_PointsToBSplineSurface` — Fit B-spline surface to points (freeform rung)

**Stage 3: Surface Reconstruction**

*Geometry (infinite surfaces and curves):*
- `Geom_Plane`, `Geom_CylindricalSurface`, `Geom_SphericalSurface`, `Geom_ConicalSurface`, `Geom_ToroidalSurface` — Analytic surface objects
- `Geom_BSplineSurface` — NURBS/B-spline surfaces
- `Geom_Line`, `Geom_Circle`, `Geom_Ellipse` — Analytic curve types
- `Geom_BSplineCurve` — Freeform curves
- `Geom2d_Curve`, `Geom2d_Line`, `Geom2d_Circle`, `Geom2d_BSplineCurve` — 2D curves for pcurves

*Surface-surface intersection:*
- `GeomAPI_IntSS` — Compute intersection curves between two surfaces

*Point-curve projection:*
- `GeomAPI_ProjectPointOnCurve` — Project point onto curve (for trimming)
- `ShapeAnalysis_Curve` — Curve analysis utilities

*Topology (B-Rep construction):*
- `TopoDS_Vertex`, `TopoDS_Edge`, `TopoDS_Wire`, `TopoDS_Face`, `TopoDS_Shell`, `TopoDS_Solid`, `TopoDS_Compound` — Topological entities
- `BRep_Builder` — Low-level topology construction (wires, faces, shells, pcurve/seam/degenerate-edge attachment — the stage-3.4/3.5 assembler)
- `BRepBuilderAPI_MakeVertex` — Create vertices
- `BRepBuilderAPI_MakeEdge` — Create edges from curves with explicit parameters and vertices
- `BRepBuilderAPI_MakeFace` — Natural-bounds faces for complete closed surfaces only (full sphere, complete torus); bounded faces are BRep_Builder-assembled

*Topology exploration:*
- `TopExp_Explorer` — Iterate over sub-shapes
- `TopExp::MapShapes` — Build maps of topology
- `BRep_Tool` — Extract geometry from topology (IsSeam, SameParameter, etc.)

*Shape healing and fixing (stage 3.6 only — face/wire/shell construction uses none):*
- `ShapeFix_Shape` — General shape repair on assembled solids
- `ShapeFix_Solid` — Solid creation/repair, `SolidFromShell`

*Shape analysis and validation:*
- `BRepCheck_Analyzer` — Validate B-Rep topology/geometry
- `ShapeAnalysis_Shell` — Shell analysis (e.g., `CheckOrientedShells`)
- `GProp_GProps` / `BRepGProp` — Compute volume, area, center of mass

**Stage 4: Output**
- `STEPControl_Writer` — Write STEP files
- `STEPControl_StepModelType` — Control STEP output mode (`AsIs`, `ManifoldSolidBrep`)
- `Interface_Static` — Set STEP header metadata

*Optional/debugging:*
- `BRepTools::Write` — Write BREP format (OCCT native, for debugging)
- `IGESControl_Writer` — Write IGES format (future enhancement)

## Architecture

### Stage 1: Mesh Input & Preprocessing

#### 1.1 Read STL File
Read the input STL file and generate an in-memory representation of the triangle mesh, with fields for future stages to traverse the mesh and fit shapes to sets of mesh faces. Weld vertices by position (with tolerance) to build connectivity.
- **Library**: OCCT `RWStl::ReadFile` (static function, via `rw_stl::read_file_charptr_progressrange_2` in the Rust bindings)
- **Input**: Binary or ASCII STL file
- **Output**: `ConnectedMesh`, storing:
    - `vertices: Vec<MeshVertex>` — Mesh vertices with double-precision 3D coordinates (`x`, `y`, `z: f64`).
    - `faces: Vec<MeshFace>` — Mesh faces with:
        - `vertex_count: u8` — 3 for triangles, 4 for quads. Always 3 after initial STL input; stage 1.3 may merge coplanar triangle pairs into quads.
        - `vertex_indices: [usize; 4]` — Indices of vertices, ordered by right-hand rule.
        - `neighbors: [i32; 4]` — Index of the mesh face across each edge, or -1 if none. Filled in stage 1.2.
        - `normal: Option<[f64; 3]>` — Mesh face normal, computed from vertices in stage 1.2. `None` for degenerate faces. (Native Rust arrays avoid OCCT binding complexity and keep the mesh data structure self-contained.)
    - `stats: MeshValidationStats` — Statistics described in stage 1.2.

Note: the mesh carries no surface-assignment state. Stage 2's face→region
assignment lives in `Stage2Output` (`face_region: Vec<usize>`), keeping
`ConnectedMesh` a pure stage-1 product. (The abandoned BFS pipeline stored
per-face hypothesis indices of each surface type on `MeshFace`; those fields
are gone.)

**Vertex welding** uses a spatial hash grid: each vertex is bucketed by integer coordinates `(round(x/tol), round(y/tol), round(z/tol))`. To find matches, adjacent cells (3×3×3 neighborhood) are checked for distance ≤ tolerance. This gives O(1) average lookup. The weld tolerance is derived from `--vertex-tolerance` as `min(1e-9, vertex_tolerance * 0.01)`, so welding is much tighter than the surface-fitting tolerance.

#### 1.2 Mesh Validation
Traverse the faces of the mesh, collecting statistics, validating the geometry, and populating normals and neighbors.
- Collect stats into `MeshValidationStats` and optionally print: `mesh_faces`, `mesh_vertices`, `mesh_edges_open` (edges with only 1 incident face), `mesh_edges_non_manifold` (edges with >2 incident faces), `mesh_edges_inconsistent_orientation`, `mesh_faces_degenerate`, `connected_shells`, `solids`, `voids_within_solids`.
- Compute and populate mesh face normals using Newell's method (handles both triangles and quads with consistent winding).
- Compute mesh face neighbors based on shared mesh edges. For each edge (vertex pair), collect all face uses. For manifold edges (exactly 2 uses), set both faces as neighbors. Also check orientation consistency: properly oriented adjacent faces should traverse the shared edge in opposite directions.
- Count connected shells via BFS over a face adjacency graph. A shell is counted as a "solid" if all its edges are manifold (exactly 2 faces per edge).
- **Repair pass** (bypassed by `--strict-mesh`): drop duplicate-vertex and
  coincident faces, stitch T-junction slivers (splitting the long-edge
  neighbor at the interior vertex), unify winding per shell, and reorient
  inverted closed shells — everything fixable without moving a vertex.
- With the `--compare` flag: check that all vertices are within `--vertex-tolerance` of the STEP shape, and that all face centroids are within `--surface-tolerance`.
```
